# Earned Trust Protocol — work queue

Single tracked queue for the assurance programme. Nothing in this file is a
claim: `DONE` means the artefact exists in Git at the named commit, `PARTIAL`
means a bounded piece exists with named residues, everything else is open.

Status vocabulary for each item: `TODO`, `IN_PROGRESS`, `PARTIAL`, `DONE`,
`BLOCKED` (with the blocker), `NOT_APPLICABLE` (with the reason).

Rules that bind this queue (from AGENTS.md, extended by the ETP mandate):

- a claim may never exceed its evidence; an implementation is not a specification;
- a test is not a proof; source-level verification is not binary-level verification;
- compatibility is not security; memory safety is not constant-time execution;
- a reproducible build is not a correct build; a signature establishes provenance,
  not truth;
- an independent agent is not an independent organisation;
- absence of observed failure is not universal absence;
- an undocumented assumption is an unresolved obligation;
- a counterexample outranks a thousand successes within its scope;
- an instrument must demonstrate sensitivity to the defect it claims to detect;
- historical evidence is never fresh evidence;
- no authority, including the project principal, may promote a contradicted claim
  by declaration;
- **do not omit, do not stop, push often.**

Persisted as `courts/phase18/timing/binsec_checkct.sh`. **Exit semantics corrected by ETP-3:** it no longer returns the tool's status on an `insecure` verdict (that was exit 0). It exits 0 only for **secure**, 1 for insecure, 2 for an invocation/format/venue refusal, 3 for silence or a truncated verdict line, 4 for unknown, 5 for a candidate digest mismatch, 6 for a tool or wall-clock failure, and 8 when `--require-secure` is not satisfied; the run is bounded by `BINSEC_WALL_TIMEOUT` and every refusal names the binary. The T16 measurements themselves are unchanged. |
| T17 | Hardware PMU counters | DONE. Hardware counter in use: the kernel msr PMU gives `perf stat -C <cpu> -a -e msr/tsc/,msr/smi/` around the real benchmark (7 931 483 421 ticks, 0 SMI, pinned to the working CPU), plus software events (task-clock 2643.02 ms at 0.999 CPUs) and userspace serialising rdtsc (2 995.047 MHz). Architectural counters are proven absent at the hardware, not just masked: CPUID.0AH reports version 0 / 0 counters, CPUID.06H.ECX[0]=0, fixed and global MSRs return EIO, and `courts/phase18/timing/pmu_msr_probe.c` programs IA32_PERFEVTSEL0 and finds it reads back 0 with PMC0 static -- the hypervisor traps and discards PMU MSR writes. Enabling them is a hypervisor change (Proxmox `--cpu host,flags=+pmu`, then stop/start); the runner takes the architectural branch automatically when CPUID.0AH reports version ≥ 1. msr/tsc counts TSC, not retired core cycles. |

Candidate DSO `a67305b1` (build ID `7cdf5b9c`), built in `openssl-rs-court-tools:1` with rustc 1.98.1 / cc 12.2.0, valgrind 3.19.0, `taskset -c 1`. **ctgrind, six cases:** `bn::arith` secret-base fold+ladder **0/0** and secret mod-inverse **0/0** (both positive controls fire at 4/1); ECDSA sign **813 061 errors / 128 contexts** (control fires; counts vary run to run because the key is generated per run, and the 128-context total is stable and matches the T12 baseline); ECDSA sign_ex **53 / 20**; RSA CRT secret moduli **98 003 / 105** (control fires). **Branch audit:** `binary_branch_audit.py` exits 0, **7/7** targets resolved uniquely and disassembled — mont core 24 jcc / 0 div, ladder 73/0, inversion 217/0, ECDSA ladder driver 113/0, RSA CRT 133/0, no `div`, no `loop`, no in-range `cpuid`, no dispatch-looking direct calls. **dudect:** `modexp` max |t| **2.47** (second run 1.58), state 1; `cmpcmp` control 3.03/state 1; `cmpnaive` control 5824.65 "definitely not constant time"; **`inverse` max |t| 485.43, state 0 — a leak verdict on that mode.** **Two results that are not clean, recorded rather than smoothed:** (1) `bn::bignum::tests::ctgrind_store_consttime_result_length` now reports **1 error / 1 context** where T8 recorded 0 — `Vec::set_len::precondition_check` → `limbs::normalise_consttime` → `store_consttime`, i.e. a debug-profile precondition check reading uninitialised tail bytes; the release-side behaviour is the branch-audit row, not this line, but the check firing is new and needs its own analysis; (2) `inverse`'s t=485 is a different-operand shape mode (2^200+1 vs 2^254−3), so it is not comparable to `modexp`'s, and it is recorded as a leak verdict on that mode rather than as evidence about the ladder. Venue deviation: the container was recreated at **6g** because `available` was 22 GiB, per `AGENTS.md` §6.1 (`min(8g, 22−16)`).  **Corrected and extended (2026-11-22): the `inverse` figure above was measured on the UNFLAGGED path.** `dudect_bn.c` never set `BN_FLG_CONSTTIME`, and the crate's `BN_mod_inverse` selects the constant-time kernel from that flag, so `inverse` was measuring variable-time inversion and its leak verdict says nothing about the kernel. The harness now has four inversion modes, each printing the flag it read back and counting operation success (a failed inversion rejects the measurement, exit 3): `inverse_vt` (unflagged, differing lengths — the control), `inverse_ct` (flagged, equal lengths), `inverse_ct_len` (flagged, differing lengths) and `inverse_ec` (flagged, modulo the P-256 order taken from `EC_GROUP_get_order`). Observed on the same DSO `a67305b1`, 20 000 measurements per batch, `taskset -c 1`: **`inverse_vt` max |t| 1002.46, state 0 (leak)** — the control reproduces the earlier finding and confirms it was the variable-time path; **`inverse_ct` 1.38, `inverse_ct_len` 1.91, `inverse_ec` 2.17, all state 1** (no leakage evidence), all 60 000/0 operations; `modexp` 2.09 and 1.05 in two runs, state 1; controls `cmpcmp` 2.04 state 1 and `cmpnaive` 7212.68 state 0. So the flagged inversion behaves like `modexp` at this sample size, and the 485 that looked like a kernel failure was the control's job. **What the harness does and does not establish:** it shows the flag was stored and the operation succeeded; *which* implementation ran is witnessed by the ctgrind case `bn::arith::tests::ctgrind_secret_mod_inverse` (0/0), not by timing. `dudect` state 1 means "no leakage evidence yet", never constant time. **Identity:** the ctgrind matrix, the branch audit and this dudect matrix are all bound to DSO `a67305b1` (build ID `7cdf5b9c`); the OpenSSH matrix cells use `ee7fa27c`, which differs because `build_trees.sh` links with `libcrypto.ld`'s version script and the timing link did not — the two are not combined into one claim. Receipt `courts/phase18/timing/RECEIPT-dudect-modes.json`; logs published under `courts/phase18/interop/logs/`.  **Identity unified (2026-11-22):** the two DSO digests that the review flagged as non-combinable were shown to differ by exactly one thing — the link script. The same archive (`62c9cf02…`) linked **with** `-Wl,--version-script=artifacts/phase2/libcrypto.ld` reproduces `ee7fa27c…` byte-for-byte, which is the DSO the four interop matrix cells use; linked without it it is `a67305b1…`, the artefact the earlier timing numbers came from. Every timing instrument was then re-run against `ee7fa27c…`: ctgrind contexts **identical** (BN 0/0 and 0/0, result-length 1/1, ECDSA 128, sign_ex 20, RSA CRT 105 on a repeat run — raw totals vary because those tests generate a key per run), dudect **verdicts identical** (`inverse_vt` 941.93 state 0; `inverse_ct` 1.36, `inverse_ct_len` 0.93, `inverse_ec` 2.15, `modexp` 1.15, all state 1), branch audit exit 0 with **7/7** resolved and the same jcc figures (24/13/73/217/113/61/133, zero divisions, zero in-range `cpuid`). So the interop result, the ctgrind matrix, the dudect matrix and the branch audit are now bound to one artefact. Receipt `courts/phase18/timing/RECEIPT-timing-unified.json`. Venue note: host `available` was 21 GiB, below the §6.1 line; the run used the already-capped 6g/6g court and records the guard as **not met** rather than silently. |

Addition, subtraction and doubling are masked selects rather than `BN_ucmp`/sign comparisons; multiplication and the Knuth D reduction run over public-width buffers, with `wrapping_*` keeping the debug profile's overflow-check branch off secret limbs as `mont_mul_into` does. A refused buffer answers `None` and the driver raises `EC_R_LADDER_STEP_FAILURE`, the authority's own answer when the `BN_CTX_get` allocations the workspace replaces are refused. Arithmetic is bit-identical: a limb-level differential over P-256/P-384/P-521 (including P-521's non-zero reduction shift) compares every operation with the generic reduced forms, and an EC-level test compares the workspace step with the unmodified `ossl_ec_GFp_simple_ladder_step` coordinate for coordinate; a predicate test pins P-256 as plain and P-384/P-521 as refused. A test also shows the workspace allocates exactly its nineteen buffers and that no field operation allocates again. `ctgrind`, ECDSA sign, same tree: **813 227 errors / 128 contexts** with the workspace forced off, **97 215 / 191** with it on (repeat runs 184–191 contexts; raw totals vary because the key is generated per run; the `CTGRIND_CONTROL=1` canary fires at `ecdsa_ossl.rs:1242`); the error volume falls 88 %, and the context rise is confined to two sites in `reduce` — the Knuth quotient-digit estimate and its add-back, the same algorithm `limbs::divide_fallible` runs and the pre-change path reached through `BN_nnmod` — plus the inlined u128 division helper, each now reported once per ladder call site rather than through the single shared `BN_mod_mul`. | T30 | Secret-bearing vector growth released old blocks before cleansing: `try_assign_limbs` used `Vec::try_reserve_exact` then cleansed only the new block, `bn_wexpand` reserved in place, and `try_boxed` dropped a refused `BIGNUM` normally. | DONE (P0 confidentiality repair): growth now allocates a separate block (`bn::limbs::{reserve_cleansed,grow_cleansed}`), copies the live limbs, cleanses the complete old allocation while still owned, then swaps and releases it through `release_cleansed`; a refused allocation leaves the original valid and untouched. `try_assign_limbs` reserves and fills a separate empty block before cleansing the destination; `bn_wexpand` uses capacity-only `reserve_cleansed` (len/value/flags untouched); `BN_consttime_swap` and `limbs::set_bit` use `reserve_cleansed`/`grow_cleansed`; `normalise_consttime` is fallible and zero-extends through `grow_cleansed`; `try_boxed` returns `Result<*mut T, T>` so `try_new_owned`/`new_owned_consttime` cleanse a refused magnitude, and both they and `store_consttime` cleanse `d` on every early failure. Test `a_forced_growth_cleanses_the_old_block_before_release` stages capacity 2, forces relocation to 4 limbs, and a test-only deallocation observer proves the old block was all-zero at release (negative control `the_reallocation_control_detects_an_uncleansed_block`). `SecretBuf`'s pre-wipe probe now counts only the initialised `[..len()]` slice and reads full capacity only after the wipe. **Open:** `parse_into`'s local `push` growth parses caller-supplied text and is classified public rather than secret; growth that happens before a vector is adopted by `SecretBuf::from_vec` is outside that owner's cleansing; `BN_CTX` pool growth moves pointers, not limbs; no Miri/valgrind was run on the new boundary (docker unavailable; host under `RLIMIT_DATA=16 GiB`). Evidence: `court/tmp/p0-realloc/NOTES.md`. |
| T31 | `runtime::obj` tests are not deterministic at the default thread count: a global `NameDb::active_walks` defers a test's removal past its own assertion when a concurrent unlocked `EVP_*_do_all` walk runs. Needs a policy decision (per-test registry instance or a scoped walk lock), not a mechanical lock. | DONE, with two separate mechanisms. (1) **Scoped walk lease.** In test builds `OBJ_NAME_do_all`/`_sorted` take a process-wide walk mutex (plus a per-thread "already held" flag, so a walk issued from a walk's own callback does not deadlock), so a test that measures the deferral boundary measures exactly one walk: `active_walks` is that walk's. (2) **Lock-depth observation.** The ordering test asked whether the registry mutex was free *at that instant*, which another test thread holding it makes false; `lock_names` now returns a guard that records this thread's lock depth in test builds, and the destructor asserts the depth is zero — the property the test states, independent of other threads. The test also waits (bounded, 30 s) for the destructor when the removal was deferred to another thread's walk, instead of assuming the two threads are ordered; the wait was necessary because the previous version observed the start of the destructor rather than its end and raced its own re-entrant lookup. Policy chosen: scoped walk lock, per this row's own note, not a per-test registry instance (the registry is genuinely process-global and the deferral is the behaviour under test). Verification: 5 consecutive full-suite runs at the default thread count (30) pass 2398/0/54, where 2 of every 3 failed before. |
| T32 | `runtime::mem::tests::array_overflow_returns_null_and_raises` failed once at the default thread count; establish whether it shares the T25 global-state mechanism. | DONE: it does share it, through a different flag. `OPENSSL_cleanup` sets the process-wide `init::STOPPED`, after which `err::with_state` refuses every call — so a concurrent `OPENSSL_cleanup` in `runtime::init::tests::cleanup_is_idempotent_and_terminal` made the overflow raise a silent no-op and `ERR_get_error` answered 0. That is the authority's own contract ("must not run concurrently with any other library call"; terminal for the process), so the defect is in the test's placement, not in the implementation: the terminal body now runs in a child process (`current_exe --exact ..._child`, gated on an env var so a whole-suite run cannot set `stopped` in the parent), and the parent asserts the child ran (`1 passed`) and exited 0. Verification: as T31, 5 consecutive default-thread-count runs clean. |

## C. OpenSSH interoperability — open work

| id | item | status |
|---|---|---|
| O1 | Choose and justify the primary version (9.9p1 backs closures/L7; 10.5p1 backs probe/unit probe); keep the other as a separate cohort | DONE: `docs/OPENSSH-COHORT.md`. **Cohort A — 9.9p1** (source sha256 `b343fbcd…`) is primary: it is the version every `closure-ssh-*` receipt names (`recipe:openssh:9.9p1`, artifact paths under `…/openssh-9.9p1/ssh`, `b48` onward), the version the `L7-functional` atlas rows carry, and the version the `SAFE_COMPATIBLE` graduation is a statement about — so O2–O11 run on it, and interoperability evidence attaches to the claim it is offered for. **Cohort B — 10.5p1** (source sha256 `d44d28a8…`) stays separate and backs the Phase-17 driver record, the CVE-2026-123158 court and the probe/unit-probe rows, with its own recorded residuals (`openssh-key-v1` RSA/ECDSA private keys rejected with `initialization error`, `utf8` unit abort from a missing `en_US.UTF-8` locale on both subjects, and the pre-10.6p2 `crypt(3)` authentication defect, which is a property of the version). **Finding:** `…/safe-c33/PROGRESS.md` says the L7 evidence "was measured on 10.5p1", which contradicts every closure receipt and atlas row; the receipts are primary, so 9.9p1 is treated as the L7 cohort and the sentence is recorded as a provenance contradiction for O12 — neither side is relabelled and no verdict changes before O12 reads the artifacts. |
| O2 | Hermetic environment: pin and verify OpenSSL source, OpenSSH tarball, PuTTY, Dropbear by digest | DONE for the pins and the verifier; two signature obligations are recorded rather than claimed. `courts/phase18/interop/MANIFEST.json` is the single place a digest is written: OpenSSH 9.9p1 `b343fbcd…` (cohort A) and 10.5p1 `d44d28a8…` (cohort B), PuTTY 0.85 `13fd4db2…`, Dropbear 2026.94 `e098034a…`, OpenSSL authority 3.6.4 `9bffaa1a…`. `courts/phase18/interop/verify_pins.py` hashes what is present (and downloads a missing required archive only with `--fetch`), never accepts a digest from a run-time checksum file, and exits 0 only when every required pin verified: observed 4/5 verified, 1 `absent-recorded` (the OpenSSL archive, already verified by `forensics/authorities/AUTHORITIES.json`), 0 failed — receipt `courts/phase18/interop/RECEIPT-o2.json`. Negative controls fire: a one-byte append to the PuTTY archive reports the expected and observed digests and exits 1; a work directory with no archives reports four `absent` and exits 1. The 9.9p1 and 10.5p1 digests re-derived from fresh downloads match the digests already recorded in `forensics/downstream/authority-baselines.jsonl` and `forensics/atlas/downstream-corpus.json`, so the historical pins are confirmed rather than replaced. **Open:** Dropbear publishes a detached `.asc` and no checksum list, so its digest is a first-fetch pin; the Dropbear and PuTTY signing keys are not pinned and neither signature is verified — both belong to O3 and neither is claimed here. |
| O3 | Install plink/puttygen/dropbear/dbclient/dropbearkey/dropbearconvert **before** configure; verify detection from the generated Makefile and `regress/test-exec.sh` rather than assuming | DONE, and the assumption in the row's own statement is false — recorded. Built from the O2 pins: PuTTY 0.85 (`plink`, `puttygen`) and Dropbear 2026.94 (`dropbear`, `dbclient`, `dropbearkey`, `dropbearconvert`), installed to one prefix; the Unix PuTTY build needs CMake, so CMake 3.31.4 is pinned in the manifest as a build tool and unpacked into the toolchain directory rather than installed system-wide. `courts/phase18/interop/build_tools.sh` performs the builds and then configures OpenSSH 9.9p1 against the pinned 3.6.4 authority. **Finding 1:** `regress/test-exec.sh` does not detect anything — it defaults to `/usr/local/bin/{plink,puttygen,dropbear,dbclient,dropbearkey,dropbearconvert}` and overrides from `TEST_SSH_*`, which `configure` fills from its `AC_PATH_PROG` results and which the generated Makefile's `interop-tests` target passes into the harness. The detection surface is therefore the Makefile's substituted values, and `verify_detection.sh` reads exactly those rather than re-running `command -v`, which would only restate the shell's PATH. **Finding 2:** Dropbear installs the server binary to `sbindir` and the clients to `bindir`; a PATH with only `bin` gives `checking for dropbear ... no` while `dbclient`/`dropbearkey`/`dropbearconvert` resolve — observed in `config.log` on the first configure, then corrected. Observed after the correction: 6/6 tools `ok`, 0 failed, `TEST_SSH_PLINK/PUTTYGEN/DROPBEAR/DBCLIENT/DROPBEARKEY/DROPBEARCONVERT` all carrying the prefix paths (`courts/phase18/interop/RECEIPT-o3.json`); `conch` is `absent` and is recorded as such, so harness tests that require it must be reported skipped, never passed. Negative controls fire: a bogus `PREFIX` reports six `mismatch` and exits 1; a Makefile with empty `TEST_SSH_PLINK`/`TEST_SSH_DROPBEAR` values reports `not_detected` and exits 1. **Open:** the OpenSSH tree is configured but not built here, and the Dropbear/PuTTY signature verification from O2 is still outstanding. |
| O4 | Build A (reference OpenSSL) and Build B (OpenSSL-RS) from the same unmodified source in isolated trees, no shared `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` | DONE, by `courts/phase18/interop/build_trees.sh`, which enforces the isolation instead of asserting it: two `cp -a` copies of the same extracted 9.9p1 tarball (`b343fbcd…`), each configured with its own `--with-ssl-dir`, and no `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` anywhere in the build. Both builds exit 0 and produce `ssh`, `sshd`, `sshd-session`, `ssh-keygen`, `ssh-agent`, `sftp-server`. A is configured against the pinned authority DSO `611e511d…`; B against a prefix whose `lib/libcrypto.so.3` is this crate's release archive linked `cc -shared` with the Phase-2 court's `libcrypto.ld` version script (`3c3a68c1…`; without that script the loader warns `libcrypto.so.3: no version information available` for any consumer built against the authority, which was observed before the fix) and whose headers are the court's generated `include/openssl`. **Finding:** neither tree's binaries carry a `RUNPATH`/`RPATH` and both name only `libcrypto.so.3` by soname, so at run time the library is chosen by the environment and a single shared `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` would hand both cells the same library with no build-time sign of it — which is why O5 resolves the mapped object rather than trusting `ldd`. `sftp-server` has no `libcrypto` dependency in this build. Receipt `courts/phase18/interop/RECEIPT-o4.json`. **Deviation:** the OpenSSH trees are built on the host (the docker daemon is unavailable), so this is an L4 build result on this host's toolchain, not the court's; the receipt records only toolchain-free facts (source digest, DSO digests, exit codes, per-binary NEEDED/RUNPATH) and the logs are in `court/tmp/o4/`. |
| O5 | Per-executable runtime identity (`ssh`, `sshd`, `sshd-session`, `ssh-keygen`, `ssh-agent`, `sftp-server`): `readelf -d`, resolved `ldd`, live `/proc/<pid>/maps`; fail closed on mixed linkage | DONE, and the third instrument had to be redesigned: `sshd` and `ssh-agent` are non-dumpable, so an external `/proc/<pid>/maps` read returns EACCES (observed). `courts/phase18/interop/verify_runtime_identity.sh` therefore asks the question at three strengths — `readelf -d` for what the binary names, `ldd` under an explicit per-cell `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` for what the loader resolves, and an `LD_PRELOAD` constructor (`courts/phase18/interop/map_probe.c`) in which **the process itself** records its own `/proc/self/maps` libcrypto lines, so the binary, the loader, the path and the mapped object are all the real ones for every executable including the short-lived ones. Observed: 20 `ok` live and resolved checks across both cells (every crypto-bearing binary maps its own cell's `libcrypto`, digests equal to that cell's DSO), 2 `no_crypto_dependency` (`sftp-server` in this build), 0 failed; receipt `courts/phase18/interop/RECEIPT-o5.json`. **Negative control fires:** cell A's `ssh` run with cell B's library path resolves to `…/b-prefix/lib/libcrypto.so.3` and is rejected as `outside_cell`, so the check is sensitive to exactly the mixed-linkage failure the item names. The first control was vacuous — it matched `ldd`'s "no version information available" warning line instead of the resolution — and was fixed by requiring ` => ` in the match. |
| O6 | Full upstream `make tests` on both builds; classify every failure as upstream-common / candidate-specific / harness; keep upstream-common failures as failures | DONE, and the suite carries two failures on **both** subjects, kept as failures. `make -k tests` in each tree (plain `make tests` stops the harness at the first failing script, so `-k` is what makes the run full — recorded as a deviation from the literal command in the item): each subject runs 46 shell-test scripts with 410 unit-test assertions, and each has the same two failing scripts, `sftp commands` and `dropbear kex`, and the same five skips (`TEST_SSH_SSHFP_DOMAIN` unset, two that need `SUDO`, conch not enabled, no PKCS#11 library). `courts/phase18/interop/summarize_tests.py` parses the two logs, refuses to fold anything into a pass, and exits non-zero unless the failure sets are equal and every failure is classified; receipt `courts/phase18/interop/RECEIPT-o6.json`. Classification: `sftp commands` fails at `lls`, which produces no output at all in either build on this host (`echo lls | sftp -D sftp-server` prints nothing), so it is upstream-common — identical on both subjects — and its cause is **not yet isolated**; `dropbear kex` fails at `diffie-hellman-group14-sha1` with an empty copy, i.e. the Dropbear 2026.94 ↔ OpenSSH 9.9p1 version pair, also upstream-common. No candidate-specific failure was observed. **Open:** those two failures remain open, not waived; the harness environment (host `ls`, Dropbear version pair) still has to be pinned before they can be closed. **Deviation:** both runs are on the host, not the court, because the docker daemon is unavailable; logs are `court/tmp/o6-{a,b}-k.log`. |
| O7 | Four-cell matrix (A-A, B-B, B-A, A-B) using the upstream harness and `TEST_SSH_SSH`, with isolated ports/config/keys per cell | **DONE**, after the first result was withdrawn. The withdrawal stands: `configure` writes an absolute `BUILDDIR`, the wrappers dropped `"$@"`, and the mapping check was set-based, so the first BA/AB cells were silently AA runs. `RECEIPT-o7.json` is now a withdrawal record (status WITHDRAWN, the claims, why each was wrong, what remains established) and the three pre-fix per-cell receipts are marked withdrawn with their successor named. **Second run, in the court**, after `cell.sh` was fixed (BUILDDIR override, `printf` wrappers, per-process mapping that aborts the cell when a side maps the wrong library, the copied `regress/.ssh/known_hosts` deleted, `SKIP_LTESTS=sftp-cmds`): all four cells are genuine — the in-process probe reports client A / server A, B / B, B / A and A / B respectively — and every cell produces **the same single failing script, `dropbear kex`** (87 ok, 7 skipped, 96 `run test` lines). **The host-key mismatch was the stale copied `known_hosts`, not a cross-library difference**: with it removed, neither BA nor AB shows a host-key or dropbear-cipher failure. `dropbear kex` is the already-classified unsupported algorithm (dbclient: `No matching algo kex`). **Venue:** the court had to rebuild both trees and the third-party tools, because the host-built binaries need `GLIBC_2.38`/`2.42`; it also needs an `sshd` privsep user, `/var/empty`, a subreaper (container PID 1 does not reap orphans, so a zombie `ssh-agent` stops `t-exec` at `agent-subprocess`) and the authority `openssl` CLI on `PATH` for `ssh2putty`. **Hazard recorded:** `t-exec` iterates `$?`, so the script set depends on mtimes — the same target ran **73** scripts on the host and **96** in the court; the two venues' counts are not comparable and a run can silently cover fewer scripts than intended. Making the selection deterministic (touch `regress/*.sh` first) is **not yet in `cell.sh`**. Logs published content-addressed under `courts/phase18/interop/logs/` and digest-linked from the receipts.  **Follow-up (2026-11-22): the venue aids are tracked now, and the selection is deterministic.** The 96-script run had depended on two files in a work directory; both are in the tree (`courts/phase18/interop/subreaper.c` — a child subreaper, because container PID 1 does not reap orphans and a zombie `ssh-agent` stalls `agent-subprocess`; `courts/phase18/interop/openssl-shim.sh` — the authority CLI `ssh2putty.sh` calls by name). `cell.sh` builds both, puts the shim on `PATH`, and **touches `regress/*.sh` before the run** so `t-exec`'s `$?` selection is maximal and repeatable. Verified by re-running cell BB: **96 `run test` lines, 87 ok, 7 `SKIPPED` + 1 `Skipped:`, 1 failure (`dropbear kex`)** — the same set as the four matrix cells, now reproducible from tracked files. **A defect was caught in the process:** the first version of the shim wrapper exec'd the shim directly, the file had no execute bit, `ssh2putty.sh` received rc 126 and wrote an empty key, and plink refused the host key (91 scripts, `failed putty transfer data`); the wrapper now invokes it through `sh` and the file is executable. |
| O8 | PuTTY client → OpenSSH server and Dropbear client → OpenSSH server; OpenSSH client → Dropbear server where the harness supports it; record negotiated algorithms from transcripts; distinguish unsupported from failed | DONE, from the harness's own transcripts, and it changes one classification. **PuTTY → sshd** (putty-transfer, putty-ciphers, putty-kex, both subjects): transfer ok at compression 0 and 1, **121 cipher/MAC combinations ok**, and **15 KEX algorithms** negotiated (including `mlkem768x25519-sha256` and `sntrup761x25519-sha512@openssh.com`). **Dropbear → sshd** (dropbear-ciphers, dropbear-kex): 9 cipher × key-type combinations ok, 3 of 4 KEX ok. The fourth cell, `diffie-hellman-group14-sha1`, is **unsupported, not failed**: the harness forces that algorithm on the server and treats any non-zero dbclient exit as a failure, but dbclient's own transcript says `No matching algo kex`, i.e. Dropbear 2026.94 does not offer it. It is recorded `unsupported` and stays counted as a failure in the upstream suite (O6); it is identical on both subjects, so it is not candidate-specific either. **OpenSSH client → Dropbear server is not covered**: the harness's dropbear scripts use dbclient as the client, so no such cell exists upstream and none is claimed. The A and B transcripts are byte-identical modulo the tree path, so every negotiated algorithm above is the same against both builds. Receipt `courts/phase18/interop/RECEIPT-o8.json` holds the verbatim algorithm lists and the dbclient transcript. **Open:** conch is not pinned, so every conch cell is a skip; the sftp `lls` failure is still unexplained (O6). |
| O9 | Coverage: Ed25519/RSA/ECDSA auth, X25519 and NIST ECDH, FFDH, PQ hybrid, AES-CTR/GCM, ChaCha20-Poly1305, HMAC integrity failures, host-key rejection, certificates, rekeying, agent, concurrent sessions | DONE as a matrix of what actually ran, with the gaps named. The O6 run was **not** the full suite: `t-exec`'s loop exits at the first failing script, so it stopped at `sftp-cmds`. Re-running with `SKIP_LTESTS=sftp-cmds` (the known upstream-common failure) runs 73 scripts; 64 print `ok`, and the two failures (`integrity`, `dropbear kex`) and five skips are identical on both subjects and identical between them line for line. Coverage, each row citing its script: **auth** `connect`/`keytype`/`keyscan`/`agent` with 109 `ed25519`, 36 `rsa-sha2-256` and 72 `ecdsa-sha2-nistp256` lines; **X25519 + NIST ECDH** `kextype` plus `curve25519`/`ecdh-sha2-nistp256` cells; **FFDH** `dhgex` plus group14/16/18 cells; **PQ hybrid** `mlkem768x25519-sha256` and `sntrup761x25519-sha512` negotiated by plink; **AES-CTR/GCM** and **ChaCha20-Poly1305** `try-ciphers`/`putty-ciphers`/`dropbear-ciphers` (37 `aes256-gcm`, 40 `chacha20-poly1305@openssh.com` lines); **certificates** `cert-hostkey` + `cert-userkey`; **rekeying** `rekey`; **agent** `agent` + `agent-timeout` + `agent-subprocess`; **concurrent sessions** `multiplex`. **HMAC integrity failures ran and failed on both subjects**: `integrity.sh` corrupts byte 2909 and expects 10 MAC errors, but with the current KEX that byte is still in the identification exchange, so the chacha20-poly1305 arm reports 0 MAC errors — an upstream test parameter that no longer lands in the encrypted stream, not a candidate defect. **Host-key rejection is NOT COVERED**: `knownhosts`, `knownhosts-command` and `hostkey-rotate` exist upstream but are not in this release's default `LTESTS` set, and are not claimed. Twenty-three further upstream scripts exist and were not run (`cert-file`, `krl`, `sshsig`, `hostbased`, `multipubkey`, `limit-keytype`, `agent-restrict`, `penalty*`, `cfginclude`, …), listed in the receipt as gaps. Receipt `courts/phase18/interop/RECEIPT-o9.json`.  **Addendum, court venue (2026-11-22):** the matrix runs in the court executed **96** scripts, not 73, and they include every row this item previously listed as not covered. `known hosts`, `known hosts command`, `hostkey rotate`, `hostkey agent`, `ssh with certificates`, `cert-file`, `key revocation lists`, `sshsig`, `hostbased`, `multiple pubkey`, `restrict pubkey type`, `authorized keys from command`, `authorized principals command`, `authinfo`, `config include`, `server config include`, `AllowUsers/DenyUsers`, `agent restrictions`, `channel timeout`, `unused connection timeout`, `sshd_config match subsystem` and `penalties` all report **ok**. **Host-key rejection is therefore covered** (`ok known hosts`), which the host-venue run could not claim. Counts for that venue: 87 ok, 7 skipped, 1 failed (`dropbear kex`), identical in all four matrix cells. **Discrepancy recorded rather than smoothed:** `integrity` **failed on the host** and **passes in the court**, on the same source; the script corrupts byte 2909 and the handshake length that byte lands in is venue-dependent, so the host failure is a test-parameter artefact of that venue, not a candidate defect — but the two venues disagree and only the court result is used for the coverage claim. The 73-vs-96 difference is the `$?` mtime hazard recorded in O7. |
| O10 | Harness negative controls: wrong client binary, wrong crypto library, failed auth, dead server, unsupported algorithm, missing third-party tool, timeout/signal kill; harness must fail if a control does not trigger | DONE: all seven controls trigger, and building them found that two of them were vacuous until fixed. `courts/phase18/interop/negative_controls.sh` runs the real harness (`make -k t-exec LTESTS=<script> BUILDDIR=<copy>`) in a throwaway copy of a built tree, mutating exactly one thing per control: `ssh` replaced by `exit 127`; `ssh` run against a zero-length `libcrypto.so.3`; `sshd` given `-o DenyUsers=*`; `sshd` replaced by `exit 1`; server forced to `diffie-hellman-group14-sha1` against a client offering only curve25519; `dbclient` moved out of the tools prefix; `sshd` started and then SIGKILLed. **A control counts only when the harness announced the script and the run failed** — a non-zero exit with no `run test` line is reported `VACUOUS`, which is what the first version produced: the mutations were invisible because `BUILDDIR` still pointed at the source tree, and three controls were then 'triggering' on setup errors rather than on the condition. Observed: 7/7 TRIGGERED, 0 vacuous (receipt `courts/phase18/interop/RECEIPT-o10.json`). **Finding:** the missing-tool control shows the harness exits **0** and prints `SKIPPED: dropbear interop tests not enabled`, a reason string that does not name the missing binary — so exit status alone can never distinguish a skip from a pass, which is why the O11 manifests count skips from the transcript and not from the exit code. **Open:** the controls are per-script, not the whole `make tests` target; the timeout control kills the server rather than the client. |
| O11 | Machine-readable manifests, exact executed/skipped/blocked counts, reproduction commands; no hand-authored totals | DONE: `courts/phase18/interop/manifest.py` derives every number from a harness log under stated rules — `executed` is a `run test` line, `ok`/`failed` are the harness's `ok`/`failed` lines (assertions, not scripts; both are reported), `skipped` is either skip dialect (`SKIPPED: reason` or the lower-case `skipped (reason)`), and `blocked` is an announced script that produced none of them. Manifests `courts/phase18/interop/MANIFEST-o11-{A,B}.json` carry the source and DSO digests, the verbatim commands, the per-script counts and the rules. Observed, identical for both subjects: **73 scripts executed, 64 ok assertions, 2 failed, 7 skipped, 0 blocked, 410 unit assertions**. The two skip dialects are why the first run of the parser reported `agent-ptrace` as `blocked`: it prints `skipped (SUDO not set)`, which the `SKIPPED:`-only rule did not recognise — the counter refusing to guess is the behaviour the item asks for, and the dialect was then added rather than the row being hand-corrected. **Open:** the manifests are per subject-run, not yet one frozen manifest per candidate; O11's `no hand-authored totals` holds for these two files, and the interop and matrix runs still carry their counts in prose in O6/O7/O9.  **Follow-up (2026-11-22):** the skip rule now accepts **any** line beginning `skipped` in any case — the harness has three spellings (`SKIPPED: reason`, `skipped (reason)`, `Skipped: running as root`), and the third is what `agent-ptrace.sh` prints, so that script is now counted as skipped rather than `blocked` (the independent review noticed exactly this one unclassified script). `manifest.py` also records the digest and byte length of the log it derived the counts from, so a manifest is self-verifying against the published bytes; regenerated manifests read 73 scripts, 64 ok, 2 failed, **7** skipped, **0** blocked, 410 unit assertions for both subjects. |
| O12 | Reconcile the historical 9.9p1/10.5p1 populations in one receipt so their results are never treated as interchangeable | DONE, and it withdraws one residual and resolves the contradiction by provenance. `courts/phase18/interop/RECEIPT-o12.json` reads both populations' primary records rather than restating them: for **9.9p1** the authority baseline (`specimen:openssh:9.9p1`, `OpenSSH_9.9p1, OpenSSL 3.6.4`, 179 imported symbols), the closure receipts' `recipe:openssh:9.9p1`, the atlas `L7-functional` rows and this campaign's O2–O11 manifests; for **10.5p1** the Phase-17 driver record (linked to the candidate DSO, sshd starts), the CVE-2026-123158 court, and the corpus record that names `d44d28a8…`. The contradiction recorded in O1 is resolved: the closure and atlas records are the primary evidence and both say 9.9p1, so the L7 cohort is 9.9p1; `PROGRESS.md`'s sentence describes the CVE court's version and attaches it to the L7 closure. The sentence is **flagged, not silently edited** — the agent that wrote the reconciliation does not rewrite its own source document in the same breath. **Correction made here:** `docs/OPENSSH-COHORT.md` had listed an `openssh-key-v1` RSA/ECDSA `initialization error` as a 10.5p1 residual; the text is in the corpus but not in the 10.5p1 record's `known_residuals`, and Phase-17 lists it as a *historical* failure fixed by `52b61567`. That residual is withdrawn as unsupported. **Open:** the two populations' results are not re-measured at one candidate here; the receipt binds what exists and forbids adding one population's counts to the other's. |

## D. ETP — assurance constitution and claim integrity

| id | item | status |
|---|---|---|
| ETP-0 | Baseline and trust-gap inventory: freeze the staging identity; inventory claims, evidence, assumptions, contradictions, missing reconstruction inputs; initial threat model; inspect binary reproducibility | PARTIAL: `S` is frozen at `9372bb28b` (clean `staging`, 13,708 tracked files) with per-tree hashes and the digest rule in `evidence/etp0/RECEIPT.md`. `evidence/etp0/CLAIMS.json` types 35 claim-bearing artefacts (`ETP0-CLM-001`…`035`) with orthogonal property/scope/quantification/identity/evidence/verdict fields: 17 `SUPPORTED`, 13 `PARTIAL`, 4 `CONTRADICTED`, 1 `SUPERSEDED`. `evidence/etp0/TRUST-GAPS.json` classifies 37 gaps: 3 asserted-without-evidence, 8 evidence-not-reconstructable, 8 identity-mismatch, 5 assumption-not-stated, 7 contradiction, 6 `UNKNOWN` with the settling evidence named. `evidence/etp0/RECEIPT.md` carries the freeze record, the initial threat model (operator error, selective publication, historical revision, artefact substitution, instrument false negative, candidate/evidence mismatch, dependency compromise, key compromise, with what each mechanism does not cover) and the reproducibility scope. **Reproducibility is stale at `S`:** the tracked recipe `forensics/tools/build_reproducibility.sh` produced byte-identical `.a`/`.so`/distribution DSO at `abb315a01` (digests `aaa32f21`/`5d0f9098`), but `S` is 40 commits and 39 source files later and the result was not re-run; the record is in untracked `court/tmp/t20/NOTES.md`, the distribution DSO named as committed is gitignored, and the `--distribution` arm's phase-2 shell source is also untracked (`.gitignore:44`). **Known-item outcomes:** O7's withdrawal was machine-readable (`WITHDRAWN` at `cc2e5fa32`) before the corrected run; 1,929 tracked references cite `court/tmp/` with 0 tracked files there; the SAFE_COMPATIBLE b85c record coexists with the `NOT ESTABLISHED` adjudication and the closure receipts' own `safe_compatible=false` (dissent preserved); T27/T29 name no receipt; and the interop set spans two build-B digests (`3c3a68c1` in O4/O5/O11, `ee7fa27c` in O7). **Outstanding:** re-run reproducibility at `S`; settle the six `UNKNOWN` items; reconcile the 1/33 versus 0/33 cohort counter; rebuild the tracked install at `S`. |
| ETP-1 | Claim algebra: typed claims with property, interpretation, quantification, scope, threat/leakage model, source/build identities, artefact digests, authority, target, assumptions, evidence, TCB, obligations, invalidation deps. Modalities (`DOCUMENTED`…`BINARY_REFINEMENT_VERIFIED`), reproduction status, verdicts — orthogonal, never a scalar score | DONE (ETP-1 evidence commit on top of `f93766a62`): `evidence/etp/claim.schema.json` is a closed JSON Schema 2020-12 type with 18 orthogonal claim axes, conditional `contradiction`/`closure`, opaque non-aggregable `measure`, and `additionalProperties: false`; `forensics/tools/etp_claims.py` is the self-contained validator and algebra (the court image has no `jsonschema`), with conjunction as the meet of member verdicts plus the union of open obligations, conservative transitive invalidation, obligation propagation gated by independently checkable exclusions, cross-claim contradiction as a hard error, and denial by default; `evidence/etp/claims.json` types all 35 claims of `evidence/etp0/CLAIMS.json` against `S = 9372bb28b` without a verdict change: 17 `ESTABLISHED`, 13 `PARTIAL`, 4 `CONTRADICTED`, 1 `SUPERSEDED`; 33 of 35 claims carry 90 open obligations (two claims are clean), 7 assumptions from the five `assumption_not_stated` gaps, and the four contradictions retain both sides with `ETP-T15` as resolution owner. `docs/ETP-CLAIM-ALGEBRA.md` is the normative description, including the ten refusals and the statement that no axis implies another; `forensics/tools/tests/test_etp_claims.py` held 40 tests before the integrity hardening (10/10 required negative controls, schema non-vacuity, positive controls, byte-identical deterministic output, algebra laws, typed-claim preservation) and holds 59 after it. Evidence: 81 cited entries, 78 digests (77 re-derived from the Git object at `S`, one from the historical commit read through `git show`); 3 paths absent at `S` (`court/tmp/t20/NOTES.md`, `court/tmp/t21/libcrypto.so.3`, `forensics/phase7_courts.py`) carry obligations rather than being silently dropped, and one historical revision is read through `git show`. Gate: court Python 3.11, `python3 forensics/tools/tests/test_etp_claims.py` → 59 tests, 0 failures; `python3 forensics/tools/etp_claims.py validate` → 35/35 claims accepted with digest re-derivation. **Hardening (2026-11-22):** an exclusion is now admitted only by a witness bound to the exact obligation, the claim's candidate identity, the verification procedure, a present result with its own re-derived `S`/`E` digest, and a reviewer whose declared role and identity differ from the claim author's (`EG_EXCLUSION_WITNESS_MISSING`/`UNBOUND`/`CANDIDATE`/`SELF_AUTHORED`/`RESULT_ABSENT`/`DIGEST`); `conjoin`, `invalidate`, `propagate` and `contradictions` run full schema, law and tree validation before answering; claim documents with duplicate object keys or non-finite numbers are refused with the offending JSON pointer (`EG_JSON_DUPLICATE_KEY`/`EG_JSON_NON_FINITE`). The suite now numbers **59 tests, 0 failures**, including end-to-end CLI controls; the committed `claims.json` still validates **35/35 with 0 refusals**, and no committed claim contains an exclusion, so **no witness was invented** for the 90 open obligations. This closes admission gaps; it promotes no claim and establishes no independence beyond declared roles. Evidence commit: the commit introducing `evidence/etp/INTEGRITY-HARDENING.md`, resolved with `git log -1 --format=%H -- evidence/etp/INTEGRITY-HARDENING.md`. **Omission-resistance hardening (2026-11-22), base `8af8870f1`:** four machinery gaps recorded by ETP-5 now have rules and controls. F1: an evidence record may carry a typed `state` (`WITHDRAWN`/`ESTABLISHED`/`NOT_ESTABLISHED`/`SUPERSEDED`/`REFUTED`/`PARTIAL`) with its artefact and revision, and a positive verdict or graduation modality over `WITHDRAWN`/`REFUTED`/`NOT_ESTABLISHED` evidence, or over no current evidence, is refused as `EG_EVIDENCE_CONTRADICTS_VERDICT`; an unknown state is an obligation, never inferred. F2: `identity.source.candidates` is schema-permitted with `candidate_relationship`, and two candidates without a stated relationship are refused as `EG_MULTIPLE_CANDIDATES` on a schema-valid document. F3: an aggregate claim must name each cell's receipt path and digest (`aggregate_cells`), refused as `EG_AGGREGATE_UNBOUND` when unbound and `EG_AGGREGATE_DIGEST_MISMATCH` when a digest disagrees with the tracked receipt; log contents are not compared. F4: a measure may be a typed `counter` or a named numeric value with a unit; equal values accept, different values refuse as `EG_CONTRADICTION_UNRESOLVED`, and a compared free-text measure refuses as `EG_MEASURE_UNTYPED`; `conjoin` returns `CONTRADICTED` for conflicting counters instead of a positive meet. Controls assert each exact identifier; the suite is **78 tests, 0 failures**, and the committed `claims.json` still validates **35/35 with 0 refusals**, so no claim loses acceptance. The gap was the rules, not the evidence. Receipt: `evidence/etp/OMISSION-RESISTANCE.md`; reproducible re-validation: `evidence/etp/omission/revalidate.py` → `evidence/etp/omission/REVALIDATION.json`. **A0-F15 repair (2026-11-23, base `86a4362c7`):** `ETP0-CLM-032` cited an `S` evidence digest (`abb8a6cb…`, the 1/33 bytes) while its `settled_by` asserted the live 0/33; the claim now cites the live record `EV-04` (`8affeac4…`, `digest_identity: E`) and retains `EV-01` as labelled history with declared state `SUPERSEDED` and recorded value 1/33, and `OBL-GAP-005` plus the verdict basis name both identities. The verdict remains `SUPERSEDED`. The algebra gains `EG_EVIDENCE_CONTRADICTS_ASSERTION` (an evidence record may carry a typed `state.measure`; a current entry whose value differs from the claim's named typed measure is refused, a non-current entry is exempt), with four controls in `test_etp_claims.py`; the suite is 535 tests and the committed document still validates 35/35 with 0 refusals. Remaining: a record that declares no measure is not compared, and other claims still share `S` while settled by later items. Record: `evidence/a0/REPAIRS.md`. |
| ETP-2 | Artefact reproduction: pristine offline builds; two independently initialised clean builds; variation matrix; resolve every byte difference or record it; no byte-normalisation to manufacture a PASS | DONE for `S'` on `staging` (working tree at HEAD `e9d492b0d`, clean at start; orchestrator commits `S'`): `evidence/etp2/reproducibility.json` and `evidence/etp2/RECEIPT.md`. **Candidate:** crate-input trees are byte-identical to `S = 9372bb28b` (`src`/`Cargo.*`/`build.rs` `51568028`, `src` `942e8975`, `courts/*`, `docker`, `tests`, `libcrypto.ld`, `AUTHORITIES.json` and the 13 `REQUIRED_CONSTITUTION` docs all match); (b) `binary_branch_audit.py` enforces byte coverage from decoded instruction lengths (`end(I_i)==start(I_i+1)`, start at the symbol, end at the boundary, byte count == extent); gaps, overlaps, truncation and overrun are refused. Qualification is not a security result. This row does not claim DO-330 qualification. |

Evidence: `evidence/haz-15/{REPORT.md,observables.json}`. **HAZ-16 progress (base `5d38094cc`):** a reproducible enumeration found 823 `guard_ffi`-wrapped mutation candidates (6,511 exported entries, 883 scripts); 79 in the nine security-relevant object owners are classified in `docs/FFI-FAILURE-ATOMICITY.md` and `evidence/haz-16/CLASSIFICATION.json` (72 `FAILURE_ATOMIC`, 3 `ROLLBACK`, 1 `POISONED`, 3 `PARTIAL_EXPOSED`). Three entries were repaired to failure-atomic publication (`BN_BLINDING_update`, `BN_BLINDING_create_param`) and remove-before-release (`OPENSSL_sk_pop_free`), each demonstrated with a test-only panic injection and a recorded revert control; three new failure-path tests and two success-path tests bring the suite to 2,430 passed / 0 failed / 54 ignored serially and at `--test-threads=4`. The register now reads `BARRIER_ABSENT=2`, `CLAIMED_NO_EVIDENCE=17`, `EVIDENCED=1`, and the FMEA rows 22 `CLAIMED_NO_EVIDENCE`, 7 `NONE`, 1 `EVIDENCED`. The barrier is `CLAIMED_NO_EVIDENCE`, not discharged: 744 candidates are `NOT_AUDITED`, three `CRYPTO_EX_DATA` rows remain partial, caller output buffers are a non-claim, and `evp`/`rsa`/`ec`/`x509` do not call `guard_ffi` (their panic path aborts at the C boundary). `OBL-PANIC-ATOMICITY` stays open and still blocks `REL-OPENSSH-B85C`. **HAZ-15 KDK correction (2026-11-22, base `5750ac150`):** the `SHA-256(decrypted block)` KDK was retracted as attacker-computable; the external-key arm now keys the synthetic answer with private CRT material or a per-key RNG secret under explicit domain separation, and the returned length is recorded as a remaining channel (64 invalid blocks, 0 collisions with the valid 8/16/32/48 lengths). `HAZ-15` returns to `CLAIMED_NO_EVIDENCE`; `OBL-RSA-EXTKEY-ORACLE` stays open. Evidence: `evidence/haz-15/{KDK-CORRECTION.md,observables.json,REPORT.md}`. **HAZ-15 follow-up (2026-11-22, base `a4cf2601a`):** the first-use secret initialisation no longer holds its side-table mutex across `RAND_priv_bytes`; a same-thread re-entry answers a named refusal, peers wait on a condition variable under a 5 s bound, and destruction during initialisation publishes nothing and wakes the waiter. The length channel is measured over 2,048 invalid blocks: every protocol-relevant length was matched at a per-ciphertext probability of order 1/246 (the 48-byte TLS length matched 9 of 2,048), so the earlier 64-block zero was a property of the sample, not a bound. `HAZ-15` stays `CLAIMED_NO_EVIDENCE`; `OBL-RSA-EXTKEY-ORACLE` stays open. Evidence: `evidence/haz-15/REENTRANCY-AND-LENGTH.md`. **HAZ-16 stack-order follow-up (base `4cafcd0ea`):** `OPENSSL_sk_pop_free` now detaches the whole element array in constant time before the first destructor, restoring the authority's first-to-last destruction order while keeping the stack empty at every callback and the path O(n); a destructor panic leaks only the not-yet-destroyed elements and a retry destroys nothing twice. Differential callback-order evidence against the pinned authority DSO, the ordering/invariant/retry controls, two revert controls and a scaling measurement are in `evidence/haz-16/STACK-ORDER.md`; the HAZ-16 barrier stays `CLAIMED_NO_EVIDENCE` and `OBL-PANIC-ATOMICITY` stays open. |

| A3 | Identity model: rule identity / logical finding identity / exact occurrence identity; fingerprints never authorise reuse across artefacts; conservative on collisions and code movement | DONE (2026-11-23, base `1925780c5`, clean `staging` at the start; orchestrator commits). `docs/FINDING-IDENTITY.md` is the normative model: `a1/v1`, with the recorded labels `a1Rule/v1`, `a1Logical/v1` and `a1Exact/v1` as its wire format (the SARIF profile's §6 remains normative for the emission, and no label changed). Rule identity is `SHA-256("a1-rule/v1" NUL ruleId)`; logical identity is `SHA-256("a1-logical/v1" NUL ruleId NUL logicalKey)` over one of **13 registered key constructors**, each declaring the native fields it uses and the location fields it excludes (a test asserts no class uses a line, column, offset, address, ordinal or call site); exact identity adds the subject URI, the `digestScope:digest` pair and the region key, and the scope fixes the algorithm (`artifact`, `source-tree` and `record` are SHA-256; `candidate-commit` is a Git object id), so a commit id is never read as file bytes. `forensics/tools/finding_identity.py` is the implementation. **Consumers:** the SARIF adapter is fully rewired (all keys, all three fingerprints, bindings, regions, exact-collision detection, and `identity_from_result`, which recomputes and refuses a tampered recorded fingerprint; the private `_fingerprint`, `_region_key` and `_normalise_text` were removed); the claim algebra uses the model's canonical JSON and its digest-form rule, refusing a Git object id recorded as a content digest as `EG_EVIDENCE_DIGEST_FORM` (row added to `docs/ETP-CLAIM-ALGEBRA.md`); the closure discipline uses the model's digests and builds row, site and target identities as positional identities marked `portable: false`; the freshness engine uses the model's digests and its Git-object/content-digest token rule. **Not adopted, with reasons in §7 of the document:** the claim algebra does not use `relate` (it relates evidence to a claim, not findings to one another), the closure discipline cannot derive a logical edge identity because `consumer_closure.py` records only positional data (deriving one would fabricate fields), and freshness reasons over artefact versions rather than occurrences while keeping its recorded `ensure_ascii=False` canonical form. **Code motion:** all eight required cases, 17 variants in `evidence/a3/CODE-MOTION.json`, produced by `classify_motion`: a move within a file, insertion above and deletion above preserve the logical identity and change the exact one; a move to another file and a file split or merge change a `path`-keyed logical identity while a location-independent register key survives; a rename changes a `path`-and-`symbol`-keyed identity; a reformat preserves the logical identity and changes the exact identity because a source-tree digest is part of it. Preservation is recomputed from recorded fields, never asserted, and `require_preservation` refuses `FI_REFUSED_UNJUSTIFIED_PRESERVATION`. **Collisions:** a colliding fingerprint is a set — every member retained, counted and listed, never merged or dropped — and `attribute` refuses `FI_REFUSED_AMBIGUOUS_ATTRIBUTION` unless exactly one member matches, including after a discriminator (a non-discriminating filter still refuses). A full scan of `evidence/a1/SARIF.json` (4 245 results, 6 runs) finds **0 exact collisions and 84 logical collision groups with 263 members** (curl `dso-class-reason` 35/130, safety `path-symbol-status`/`path-symbol-class` 49/133), because those keys exclude the native site or the call-site addresses; the exact identities separate every member, the groups are recorded in `evidence/a3/COLLISIONS.json`, and re-keying was not done because it would make the key candidate-bound. **Reuse:** `relate` requires one of four stated relationships and refuses `FI_REFUSED_CROSS_ARTEFACT_REUSE`, `FI_REFUSED_CROSS_CANDIDATE_REUSE`, `FI_REFUSED_UNSTATED_RELATIONSHIP` and `FI_REFUSED_INSUFFICIENT_RELATIONSHIP`; a logical match across artefacts or candidates authorises logical scope only and cannot transfer a suppression, discharge, receipt or cache entry. **Migration:** the recorded fingerprints are reproduced, not regenerated — re-projecting the six native records in memory under the generalised module gives **12 735 of 12 735 fingerprints equal, 0 mismatches, an equal fingerprint-stream digest, no run-property difference other than the adapter's own digest, and no result-object difference**; `evidence/a1/SARIF.json` (`5c0cfee6…`, 13 651 450 bytes) is unchanged and `evidence/a3/MIGRATION.json` binds the old adapter digest `35614b90…`, the new module digest, both streams and the comparison. | A5 | Completeness and execution admission: expected vs examined population, unsupported constructs, skips, timeouts, truncation; an absent population is not an empty population; exit code is not completeness | DONE (2026-11-24, base `d7fb15776`, clean `staging` at the start; orchestrator commits). `docs/COMPLETENESS-ADMISSION.md` is the normative document and `forensics/tools/completeness.py` the implementation; the controls are `forensics/tools/tests/test_completeness.py` and the recorded evidence is `evidence/a5/{POPULATIONS,ADMISSION,CONTROLS}.json`, produced deterministically by `evidence/a5/build_evidence.py`. **The rule:** a claim of completeness requires an expected population stated by a record, an examined count equal to it, no omitted members, and every examined member identified; anything weaker is admitted at the weaker state it supports. **Six base states** (`COMPLETE`, `SAMPLED`, `TRUNCATED_BOUNDED`, `ABSENT`, `EMPTY`, `UNKNOWN`) and two further weaker states the records actually need (`AGGREGATE_ONLY`, `DEFERRED`); adding them narrows what may be claimed and does not widen the definition of complete. `ABSENT` and `EMPTY` are distinct in both directions, a declaration of `COMPLETE` over a zero population is admitted as `EMPTY`, and `UNKNOWN` is never admitted as `COMPLETE`. **Sixteen stable refusal codes**, each documented with the wrong conclusion it prevents: `CA_REFUSED_ABSENT_AS_EMPTY`, `CA_REFUSED_EMPTY_AS_ABSENT`, `CA_REFUSED_ABSENT_AS_OBSERVED`, `CA_REFUSED_UNRECORDED_POPULATION`, `CA_REFUSED_EXIT_AS_COMPLETENESS`, `CA_REFUSED_OMITTED_UNRECORDED`, `CA_REFUSED_TRUNCATION_AS_COMPLETE`, `CA_REFUSED_SAMPLE_AS_CENSUS`, `CA_REFUSED_UNKNOWN_AS_COMPLETE`, `CA_REFUSED_POPULATION_DRIFT`, `CA_REFUSED_COUNT_INCONSISTENT`, `CA_REFUSED_DUPLICATE_MEMBER`, `CA_REFUSED_MEMBER_UNIDENTIFIED`, `CA_REFUSED_SAMPLE_WITHOUT_POPULATION`, `CA_REFUSED_STATE_UNDECLARED`, `CA_REFUSED_STATE_CONTRADICTED`. The reader refuses a missing field rather than defaulting it: a missing presence field is never read as present and a missing expected count is never read as zero. a scope broader than its target is refused, and a logical target used for an exact occurrence is refused. **Five dispositions** (`ACTIVE`, `EXPIRED`, `INVALIDATED`, `NON_ACTIONABLE`, `PENDING_REVIEW`), each keeping its finding in the population, with an `ACTIVE` suppression licensing an accepted disposition and not truth. **Seven invalidation triggers**, each returning a status of `FIRED`, `NOT_FIRED` or `NOT_RUN`, a new disposition and a reason citing the identity that changed: `candidate-identity-change`, `source-tree-digest-change`, `tool-identity-change`, `expiry` (to `EXPIRED`), `target-absent-from-population`, `new-occurrence-at-logical-identity` and `policy-basis-change`; exact identity is `a1Exact/v1`, refined with the native locator and message only where it collides, as the profile requires. **17 stable codes, 17 controls**, each firing its named code; the copied `run.properties.a1.projection` is the parent run's and is stated as such, not the shard's. **Non-claims:** a shard is not a census, an upload is not an admission, and GitHub's ingestion/size ceiling/schema validation are `NOT_RUN` and not evidence this project controls. |

the digest is computed by reading the bytes, never caller-supplied; a Git object id, a short token, a non-hex string, an absent file and a duplicate name are each refused; the predicate's `record` block must name a subject at that subject's digest. **Registry (5 types):** `.../population/v1` (A5 admission), `.../baseline-delta/v1` (A7 delta), `.../sarif-emission/v1` (A1/A8 log or shard), `.../instrument-qualification/v1`, `.../suppression/v1` (A6); A5 `evidence/a5/ADMISSION.json` `d4871ec9…` `a1-sarif-results` `COMPLETE` 4,245 examined / 0 omitted; A7 `evidence/a7/DELTA.json` `0444a879…` `a3-fingerprint-migration` 4,245 records / **0 `RESOLVED`** / admitted; A8 `evidence/a8/SHARD-000.sarif` `b16d376a…` 6 runs / 4,245 results / `subset:true` / parent 4,245. **Seven records cannot be attested, with the measured code:** `t4-suppressions` and `t11-satisfied-runs` (`IT_REFUSED_RECORD_INADMISSIBLE`: their own declarations are refused), `o11-interop-scripts` (`IT_REFUSED_CLAIM_UPGRADE`: the HAZ-15 population is `UNKNOWN`), `curl-residual-sites` as `COMPLETE` (`IT_REFUSED_CLAIM_UPGRADE`: `TRUNCATED_BOUNDED`, 200 of 449), `a3-fingerprint-migration` as `RESOLVED=1`, `timecop-supercop` as verified (`RECORDED`), and `t4-suppressions` as `ACTIVE` (no expiry; the record is `NON_ACTIONABLE`). **Controls:** 17 over the 12 codes, one per code and one per upgrade; the producer reads each code from the tool. **Boundary:** A10 owns DSSE and PAE; a bare statement is not a signed attestation, not a proof, not a verification and not admission. **NOT_RUN:** DSSE/PAE, signatures and trust roots (A10/A12/A14–A17), A11 parser hardening, JSON-Schema validation, a genuine SLSA statement, multi-subject statements, independent review, BOUNDARY and SEAL. Receipt: `evidence/a9/RECEIPT.md`. |
| A10 | DSSE authentication with the exact PAE over the received bytes; never reparse/reserialise before verification; `keyid` is an unauthenticated hint only | DONE (2026-11-24, base `da021a765`, clean `staging` at the start; orchestrator commits). `docs/DSSE-PAE.md` is the normative document and `forensics/tools/dsse.py` the implementation; `forensics/tools/tests/test_dsse.py` is 44 controls; `evidence/a10/{ENVELOPES/,ENVELOPES.json,CONTROLS.json,PAE-VECTOR.txt}` are the records, produced deterministically by `evidence/a10/build_evidence.py`. **The rule:** verification is over the bytes that were received; the payload is never parsed before the signature check and never re-encoded, because any whitespace, key-order, escaping or number-form difference is a different statement. **The PAE, exactly:** `DSSEv1` SP decimal(UTF-8 byte length of the type) SP type SP decimal(body length) SP body, no padding, no leading zeros; the specification vector `DSSEv1 29 http://example.com/HelloWorld 11 hello world` is asserted by the producer, the tests and the index, with empty-type (`DSSEv1 0  0 `), empty-body and multi-byte UTF-8 type cases fixed; `parse_pae` is the strict inverse and refuses a wrong declared length, a leading zero or bytes after the body. **Order:** parse the envelope only with duplicate-key detection at every object; refuse trailing bytes; decode canonical standard base64 strictly (wrong alphabet, padding, non-zero pad bits and trailing data refused); compute the PAE from the received type and decoded bytes and require the PAE body to be those bytes; call the caller's verifier; only then, when a statement is required, decode the verified payload through A9; return the received bytes. Every re-serialisation is refused, and a signature that verifies only over a canonical re-encoding is named as such, never accepted. **Envelope contract:** exactly `payloadType`, `payload`, `signatures`; unknown members refused (forward extensions are A11's); each signature exactly `keyid` and `sig`; `keyid` is an unauthenticated hint this module never uses to select a key. **13 stable codes:** `DS_REFUSED_ENVELOPE_INVALID`, `DS_REFUSED_DUPLICATE_KEY`, `DS_REFUSED_PAYLOAD_TYPE_MISSING`, `DS_REFUSED_PAYLOAD_TYPE_MISMATCH`, `DS_REFUSED_BASE64_NONCANONICAL`, `DS_REFUSED_PAYLOAD_ABSENT`, `DS_REFUSED_SIGNATURES_ABSENT`, `DS_REFUSED_SIGNATURE_INVALID`, `DS_REFUSED_PAE_RESERIALISED`, `DS_REFUSED_PAE_LENGTH`, `DS_REFUSED_PAYLOAD_NOT_STATEMENT`, `DS_REFUSED_TRAILING_BYTES`, `DS_REFUSED_OBJECT_SUPPLIED`; a parsed `dict`/`list`/`str` is refused because it has lost the received byte identity. **Four real envelopes, one per A9 statement,** each signing that statement's exact committed bytes under `application/vnd.in-toto+json` with a test-only HMAC-SHA256 key derived from a fixed public seed (`test-only:hmac-sha256:8e10a5a15d233d54`): `a1-sarif` payload `d53bc4eb…` envelope `8b0aa798…`; `a5-admission` `d17bb4c9…` / `8f9144f2…`; `a7-delta` `d4dfa5a3…` / `068ea127…`; `a8-shard-000` `ea67963a…` / `5bf418cc…`; every payload digest equals the A9 statement digest and the verified statement digest, so the signature is over the bytes A9 emitted. **13 controls, one per code**, built in memory from a real envelope and asserting the code read from the tool, including a whitespace-different re-serialisation, a wrong PAE length and a parsed object supplied instead of bytes. **What the envelopes establish:** framing, PAE over the received bytes and a signature that verifies under the test-only key. **What they do not:** authenticity (a test key is not a trust root), identity or authorisation (A14), transparency (A16/A17), independent verification (A12), or that the A9 statements are true or current. **Boundary:** this module makes no trust decision; key selection, distribution and identity are A12/A14/A16's; a bare envelope is not a signed attestation. **Gates:** 44 new `unittest` controls pass; the whole tools discovery is 1,047 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors (1,003 before this item; none removed, none ignored); `docs_consistency.py` and `spec_guard.py check` are clean; all JSON and all four envelopes pass `python3 -m json.tool`; the producer is byte-identical across two runs and against the committed bytes; `dsse.py verify` exits 0 on all four envelopes and prints `DS_OK`. `free -g` reported 23 GiB available, the derived cap was `min(8g, 23 − 16) = 7g` above the 2 GiB floor, and the already-running 6 GiB court was reused; no build ran. **NOT_RUN:** A12's independent cryptographic verification and trust root, A14–A17 role, threshold, bundle and transparency rules, A11 parser hardening, a production signer or real key, subject re-hashing on the statement path, JSON-Schema validation, independent review, BOUNDARY, SEAL and the Rust suite (`src/` was not modified). Receipt: `evidence/a10/RECEIPT.md`. |
| A11 | DSSE parser hardening: duplicate JSON keys, invalid encodings, oversized payloads, signature-count bounds, algorithm downgrade refusal, forward-extension discipline | DONE (2026-11-24, base `f1c1a535d`; population re-frozen 2026-11-25 at `e94c7fdf1`, superseding the `b26552e21` declaration with membership unchanged at 506 and counts unchanged, because the A16 records and `evidence/etp-t14/ARTICLES.json` moved in content only; clean `staging` at the start; orchestrator commits). `docs/PARSER-HARDENING.md` is the normative document and `forensics/tools/harden.py` the implementation; `forensics/tools/tests/test_harden.py` is 64 controls; `evidence/a11/{SCAN.json,DIFFERENTIAL.json,CONTROLS.json}` are the records, produced deterministically by `evidence/a11/build_evidence.py`. **The rule:** a received evidence record is read by one strict reader whose refusals are named, or it is not read; a construct whose meaning depends on the reader is refused, not normalised. **The strict reader:** `loads(bytes, max_bytes, max_depth, integer_bits)`; UTF-8 strict and a BOM refused; a duplicate key at any depth refused (not last-wins); only object/array/string/number/`true`/`false`/`null`; `NaN`/`Infinity`/`-Infinity` and every other bare literal refused; depth bounded while parsing and size bounded before it; integers bounded by `integer_bits`; non-canonical integer forms (`-0`, a leading zero, a leading `+`, `1.`, `.5`) refused; trailing data refused; a lone surrogate and a raw control character refused; object keys must be text. **13 stable codes:** `PH_REFUSED_ENCODING`, `PH_REFUSED_BOM`, `PH_REFUSED_DUPLICATE_KEY`, `PH_REFUSED_DEPTH`, `PH_REFUSED_SIZE`, `PH_REFUSED_NONFINITE`, `PH_REFUSED_TRAILING_DATA`, `PH_REFUSED_INTEGER_RANGE`, `PH_REFUSED_NUMBER_FORM`, `PH_REFUSED_LONE_SURROGATE`, `PH_REFUSED_CONTROL_CHARACTER`, `PH_REFUSED_KEY_NOT_TEXT`, `PH_REFUSED_SYNTAX`, each with the wrong conclusion it prevents. **Differential:** `loads_permissive` is the explicitly named standard-library reference (`utf-8-sig`, `raw_decode` ignoring trailing bytes, no duplicate-key hook, `strict=False`, the default `parse_constant`); `differential` records the strict result, the permissive result, a duplicate-key collapse and every number whose type or value moved, and `differential_pair` reports `samePermissiveValue`. **Decisive case:** `{"a":1,"a":2}` (`1c53ee0d…`) and `{"a":2}` (`7e8059f4…`) are distinct byte strings; the permissive reader returns `{"a":2}` for both and the strict reader refuses the first with `PH_REFUSED_DUPLICATE_KEY`. The `-0`/`0` pair collapses with `PH_REFUSED_NUMBER_FORM`, `1`/`1.0` records the type move, and the non-finite, BOM, trailing-data and depth pairs are recorded with both digests. **Scan:** a population frozen at `e94c7fdf1` (re-frozen from `b26552e21` with the same membership) over the glob `evidence/*.json` (`fnmatch` over `git ls-files`; `evidence/a11/` excluded so the record cannot depend on its own bytes). The record lists all 506 `{path, sha256}` members and reproduces at later commits; files added afterwards are reported as `NEW_SINCE_RECORD` and never absorbed: **506 files, 331 examined at or below 4 MiB, 331 `STRICT_OK`, 175 `SKIPPED_SIZE`** (reported, not omitted; was 386 / 211 / 211 / 175 at `f1c1a535d`, where the population was 2.15 GB), **0 refusals**. **Controls:** 13 over the 13 codes, one each, with the size, depth and integer bounds tested at the exact limit and one past it. **Boundary:** this hardens a reader for evidence files; it does not harden the Rust parsers under `src`, which have their own tests, and it does not claim the C or Rust parser is safe. **Gates:** 64 new `unittest` controls passed at the original batch (the population correction added five, taking the focused module to 69); the whole tools discovery was 1,111 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors at the original batch (1,047 before this item; none removed, none ignored; the population correction measured 1,202 with the three documented `test_harden` drift failures, and the re-freeze is what repairs them); `docs_consistency.py` and `spec_guard.py check` are clean; every created JSON passes `python3 -m json.tool`; the producer is byte-identical across two runs and against the committed bytes; `harden.py scan 'evidence/*.json' --frozen` prints `files=506 examined=331 strictOk=331 skippedSize=175 refused=0` (was `files=386 examined=211 strictOk=211 skippedSize=175 refused=0` at `f1c1a535d`) with `newSinceRecord` reported separately, and exits 0. `free -g` reported 23 GiB available, the derived cap was `min(8g, 23 − 16) = 7g` above the 2 GiB floor, and the already-running 6 GiB court was reused; no Rust build ran. **NOT_RUN:** the Rust parsers under `src` and the Rust suite, the C parser and the authority, records above the 4 MiB bound, a second implementation of the reader, every other JSON reader, independent review, BOUNDARY, SEAL and any cryptographic property. Receipt: `evidence/a11/RECEIPT.md`. |
| A12 | Independent cryptographic verification of envelopes: the root of trust must not be the unverified candidate; keep verification deps out of `libcrypto`/`libssl` | DONE (2026-11-24, base `1d571da35`, clean `staging` at the start; orchestrator commits). `docs/INDEPENDENT-VERIFICATION.md` is the normative document and `forensics/tools/independent_verify.py` the implementation; `forensics/tools/tests/test_independent_verify.py` is 38 controls; `evidence/a12/TRUST-ROOTS.json`, `evidence/a12/VERIFICATION.json` and `evidence/a12/CONTROLS.json` are the records, produced deterministically by `evidence/a12/build_evidence.py`. **The rule:** an envelope is independently verified only when the verifier's root of trust is outside the candidate, outside the producing party, and selected by a recorded policy rather than by the envelope; a symmetric signature can never establish independence because the verifier holds the same secret as the signer. **Trust-root contract:** keyid, algorithm, publicMaterial or an explicit symmetric flag, provenance (path and sha256), trustDomain, independenceBasis and recordedAt; the inventory is a separate tracked record and may not be read from the candidate evidence being verified. **Policy selection:** the policy names the keyid; the envelope's keyid is an unauthenticated hint recorded for comparison only; if the policy names none, `IV_REFUSED_KEY_FROM_ENVELOPE` fires. **Nine checks, fifteen stable codes:** policy selection (`IV_REFUSED_KEY_FROM_ENVELOPE`), symmetric root (`IV_REFUSED_SYMMETRIC`), candidate provenance (`IV_REFUSED_CANDIDATE_TRUST_ROOT`), unrecorded, digest, stale and multiple roots (`IV_REFUSED_TRUST_ROOT_UNRECORDED`, `IV_REFUSED_TRUST_ROOT_DIGEST_MISMATCH`, `IV_REFUSED_TRUST_ROOT_STALE`, `IV_REFUSED_MULTIPLE_ROOTS`), same domain (`IV_REFUSED_SAME_DOMAIN`), self-issued basis (`IV_REFUSED_SELF_ISSUED`), signature over the received PAE (`IV_REFUSED_SIGNATURE`), payload and artefact subject digest (`IV_REFUSED_PAYLOAD_NOT_SUBJECT`, `IV_REFUSED_SUBJECT_ABSENT`), artefact naming (`IV_REFUSED_SUBJECT_MISMATCH`), plus `IV_REFUSED_ENVELOPE` and `IV_REFUSED_NO_PUBLIC_VERIFIER`; a caller-supplied public verifier is mandatory and no signature primitive is implemented, so no verifier is `NOT_RUN`, never a pass. **Outcomes:** `INDEPENDENTLY_VERIFIED`, `SELF_ISSUED`, `UNVERIFIED` and `NOT_RUN`; `SELF_ISSUED` is never counted as a pass. **Real result:** the inventory records one root, the A10 test-only HMAC-SHA256 key, with symmetric true, trustDomain same-party and provenance inside the candidate; the four real envelopes are **0 independently verified and 4 self-issued**, with `IV_REFUSED_SYMMETRIC`, `IV_REFUSED_CANDIDATE_TRUST_ROOT` and `IV_REFUSED_SELF_ISSUED` each; the absence of an external root is stated explicitly rather than left as an empty list. **Controls:** 15 over the 15 codes, one each, plus a control-only stub-verifier positive control that reaches `INDEPENDENTLY_VERIFIED` under a distinct public-key root and establishes the decision procedure, not a real independent party; it is excluded from the real aggregate. **Gates:** 38 new `unittest` controls pass; the whole tools discovery is 1,149 tests, 0 failures (1,111 before this item; none removed, none ignored); `docs_consistency.py` and `spec_guard.py check` are clean; every created JSON passes `python3 -m json.tool`; the producer is byte-identical across two runs and against the committed bytes; `independent_verify.py verify` exits 1 on all four envelopes with `SELF_ISSUED` and the three codes; | A13 | Signature reproducibility vs artefact reproducibility kept separate; never seed production randomness for reproducible signatures | DONE (2026-11-24, base `6b33f1caa`, clean `staging` at the start; orchestrator commits). `docs/REPRODUCIBILITY-AXES.md` is the normative document and `forensics/tools/reproducibility.py` the implementation; `forensics/tools/tests/test_reproducibility.py` is 53 controls; `evidence/a13/{SIGNATURE.json,ARTEFACT.json,CLASSIFICATION.json,CONTROLS.json}` are the records, produced deterministically by `evidence/a13/build_evidence.py`. **The rule:** a deterministic signature and a reproducible build are different claims; the signature axis is a property of the scheme and its inputs, the artefact axis is a property of the build, and neither implies the other. **Scheme registry (4):** `hmac-sha256` (deterministic, symmetric), `rsa-pkcs1v15` (deterministic, asymmetric), `ed25519` (deterministic, asymmetric), `ecdsa` (randomised, asymmetric); deterministic schemes compare by byte equality, randomised schemes by non-equality plus a nonce-reuse check. **Signature states:** `SIGNATURE_STABLE`, `SIGNATURE_VARIES` (expected for a randomised scheme), `SIGNATURE_NONCE_REUSED` (a refusal), `SIGNATURE_UNKNOWN`. **Artefact states:** `ARTEFACT_REPRODUCIBLE`, `ARTEFACT_DIFFERS`, `ARTEFACT_NOT_REBUILT`, `ARTEFACT_UNKNOWN`; an absent rebuild record is `ARTEFACT_NOT_REBUILT`, never `REPRODUCIBLE`. **14 stable codes:** `SR_REFUSED_SIGNATURE_AS_ARTEFACT`, `SR_REFUSED_ARTEFACT_AS_SIGNATURE`, `SR_REFUSED_DETERMINISTIC_SCHEME_AS_INDEPENDENCE`, `SR_REFUSED_NONDETERMINISTIC_AS_FAILURE`, `SR_REFUSED_NONCE_REUSE`, `SR_REFUSED_ENVELOPE_DIGEST_AS_BUILD_DIGEST`, `SR_REFUSED_SEED_AS_KEY`, `SR_REFUSED_INPUTS_UNSTATED`, `SR_REFUSED_REBUILD_NOT_RUN`, `SR_REFUSED_SCHEME_UNRECORDED`, `SR_REFUSED_REPLAY_AS_REPRODUCTION`, `SR_REFUSED_ATTEMPTS_INSUFFICIENT`, `SR_REFUSED_ATTEMPT_MALFORMED`, `SR_REFUSED_DETERMINISTIC_VARIED`; nonce reuse is a key-recovery defect, not reproducibility. **Real signature result:** the four A10 envelopes are signed twice over the same A9 statement bytes with the same `test-only:hmac-sha256:8e10a5a15d233d54` key; every pair is byte-identical and every attempt reproduces the committed A10 envelope digest (`8b0aa798`, `8f9144f2`, `068ea127`, `5bf418cc`), so `SIGNATURE_STABLE` is 4/4. This is a property of HMAC-SHA256 and of the fixed public test seed; it says nothing about the build, authenticity or independence. **Real artefact result:** the four A1/A5/A7/A8 artefacts have no rebuild record and the A4 DSO (`artifacts/phase2/install/lib/libcrypto.so.3`, `fd25cc9e6424...`) has a recorded rebuild whose outcome is `NOT_RUN` (22 GiB available is below the 24 GiB floor; the court is 6 GiB and `build_reproducibility.sh` exits 101), so every one of the 5 subjects is `ARTEFACT_NOT_REBUILT`, 0 reproducible, and **no build-reproducibility claim is made**; a stable digest is an identity, not a reproduction. **Classification:** the four real pairs are `STABLE_SIGNATURE__NOT_REBUILT_ARTEFACT` with `SR_REFUSED_SIGNATURE_AS_ARTEFACT` and `SR_REFUSED_REBUILD_NOT_RUN` named as prohibited; the DSO is `UNPAIRED` because it is a build product, not a signed statement. **Controls:** 14, one per code; the synthetic randomised-scheme pair that varies is accepted, the pair that repeats is refused as `SR_REFUSED_NONCE_REUSE`; the synthetic reproducible artefact is `control-only` and is excluded from the real result. **Gates:** 53 new `unittest` controls pass; whole discovery is 1,202 tests, **3 failures and 0 errors**: the three are in `test_harden` because A11's committed `evidence/a11/SCAN.json` records 386 files while its `evidence/*.json` population is now 389 after A12 committed its three JSON records (pre-existing at this base, recorded here and not relaxed; `docs_consistency.py` and `spec_guard.py check` are clean; every created JSON passes `python3 -m json.tool`; the producer is byte-identical across two runs and against the committed bytes; `reproducibility.py classify` exits 0 and prints every pair. `free -g` reported 22 GiB available and the already-running 6 GiB court was reused; | A14 | Role-bound authorisation: maintainer, source/build/analysis/proof producer, instrument reviewer, adjudicator, external reproducer, release authority, revocation authority; wrong-role signature insufficient | DONE (2026-11-24, base `4e9262bf3`, clean `staging` at the start;
