`evidence/a14/{ROLES.json,AUTHORISATION.json,CONTROLS.json}` are the records, produced deterministically by `evidence/a14/build_evidence.py`; `evidence/a14/RECEIPT.md` is the receipt. **The rule:** a valid signature authorises nothing until the signer's identity holds the role the action requires, at the action's scope, at the action time, with the required separation of duties; an unverified signature, a wrong-role signature, an unknown or unassigned role, a stale/revoked/out-of-scope assignment, the producer authorising its own promotion, one identity on both sides of a separation, and one identity counted twice are each refused. **Seven roles:** `maintainer`, `producer`, `instrument_reviewer`, `adjudicator`, `external_reproducer`, `release_authority`, `revocation_authority`; the producer of the evidence, the instrument's author and the sole author of a release are the `producer` identity in that action's context. **Six actions with policy:** `release` requires `release_authority`+`adjudicator`, 2 distinct signers, `release_authority`≠`producer`/`adjudicator`; `claim-promotion` requires `adjudicator`, `adjudicator`≠`producer`, producer may not sign; `consumer-graduation` requires `adjudicator`+`external_reproducer`, 2 distinct, all three pairwise separations; `instrument-qualification` requires `instrument_reviewer`≠ instrument author; `evidence-correction` requires `maintainer`≠`producer`; `revocation` requires `revocation_authority`≠`producer`. **Order:** action known → signature verified (before its role) → identity recorded → role known → role assigned at the action time (expiry/revocation) → scope → required role → self-authorisation → separation → distinct signers. **13 stable codes:** `RA_REFUSED_ROLE_UNKNOWN`, `RA_REFUSED_ROLE_NOT_ASSIGNED`, `RA_REFUSED_WRONG_ROLE`, `RA_REFUSED_SELF_AUTHORISATION`, `RA_REFUSED_SEPARATION`, `RA_REFUSED_IDENTITY_UNRECORDED`, `RA_REFUSED_SIGNATURE_UNVERIFIED`, `RA_REFUSED_SCOPE_EXCEEDED`, `RA_REFUSED_EXPIRED`, `RA_REFUSED_REVOKED`, `RA_REFUSED_INSUFFICIENT_SIGNERS`, `RA_REFUSED_DUPLICATE_SIGNER`, `RA_REFUSED_ACTION_UNKNOWN`. **Real result:** `evidence/etp-t8/ASSIGNMENTS.json` establishes five real fills: `P-CAMPAIGN` producer (author 50, evidence_producer 50, proof_producer 15, instrument_author 15), `P-CAMPAIGN` adjudicator (50/50), `P-CAMPAIGN` external_reproducer (A-ADJ-B85C, same-party and not external), `P-PRINCIPAL` release_authority (A-REL-OPENSSH-B85C) and `P-CAMPAIGN` revocation_authority; both tokens are undeclared and mapped to `P-PROJECT-UNDECLARED`; 0 declared identities, 0 external trust domains, the only signing root is the A10 test-only HMAC-SHA256 key (same-party, signs statements, not authorisations), and `maintainer`, `instrumentReviewer`, `externalReproducer`, `releaseAuthority` are each recorded `none`. | A18 | Survivable key compromise (TUF principles): rotation, delegation, expiry, revocation, anti-rollback, anti-freeze; old signatures never authorise a stale release | DONE (2026-11-24, base `2e8e9aa65`, clean `staging` at the start; orchestrator commits). `docs/KEY-LIFECYCLE.md` is the normative document, `forensics/tools/key_lifecycle.py` the implementation, `forensics/tools/tests/test_key_lifecycle.py` 49 controls, and `evidence/a18/{INVENTORY,EVALUATION,CONTROLS}.json` the records, produced deterministically by `evidence/a18/build_evidence.py`. **The rule:** an old signature never authorises a stale release. **The model:** metadata for `root`, `targets`, `snapshot`, `timestamp` and `delegation`, each with role, version, expiry, threshold, keys and signatures plus the role's chain reference; `snapshot` names the `targets` version and digest, `timestamp` names the `snapshot` version and digest, and `targets` declares each delegation by name and digest. Signatures are a **structural test-only digest model** (a `sha256:` value over the canonical signed body and keyid), not cryptography. **Evaluation order:** repository shape, root, targets, snapshot, timestamp, delegations, releases, then the hash chain and freeze. **Rotation:** the new root must be version-greater and signed by a threshold of both the old and the new root keys. **Rollback:** root strictly increases; targets, snapshot and timestamp may not decrease. **Expiry and freeze:** metadata not expiring after `now` is refused, and an unexpired timestamp older than the declared maximum refresh interval is a freeze (exactly the interval is accepted, one second past is refused). **Revocation:** an expired, not-yet-valid, revoked, unknown or wrong-role key is refused, and a revoked key's verifying signature is refused. **Delegation:** must be present, declared, hash-bound and unexpired. **Stale release:** a release behind the accepted targets version, or signed by a key revoked at or before its recorded time, is refused. **21 stable codes**, each documented with the wrong conclusion it prevents. **The real inventory is one key and no repository.** `INVENTORY.json` records the single A10 test-only HMAC-SHA256 key `test-only:hmac-sha256:8e10a5a15d233d54`, `symmetric: true`, with no expiry, no revocation record, no metadata version and no delegation; `repositories: 0`, 0 metadata versions, 0 revocation records, 0 expiry records and 0 delegations. The finding is explicit: the key is a **single point of compromise** with no detectable recovery, because there is no second root, no revocation list and no metadata version to distinguish a pre-compromise signature from a post-compromise one; a bundle or envelope signed before a hypothetical compromise is **indistinguishable** from one signed after it. `EVALUATION.json` therefore records every role and the stale-release check as `NOT_RUN` with `TK_NOT_RUN`: **0 repositories and nothing accepted**, and `NOT_RUN` is not a pass. **Controls:** `CONTROLS.json` holds 22 `control-only` synthetic repositories over the 21 codes, including the required root rotation signed only by the new root, targets version decrease, snapshot and targets hash mismatches, expired timestamp, a timestamp frozen past the interval, a revoked key whose signature verifies, an expired delegation, and a release behind the accepted version; | A19 | Authenticated corrections and retractions: append-only, bound to the original attestation, propagating invalidation; preserve what was believed and what was disproved | DONE (2026-11-24, base `6bf8c80aa`, clean `staging` at the start; orchestrator commits). `docs/CORRECTIONS.md` is the normative document, `forensics/tools/corrections.py` the implementation, `forensics/tools/tests/test_corrections.py` 51 controls, and `evidence/a19/{EVENTS,PROPAGATION,STATUS,CONTROLS}.json` the records, produced deterministically by `evidence/a19/build_evidence.py`. **The rule:** a correction is a new bound event; the original `assert` is never edited; everything that cited the superseded digest is invalidated; both what was believed and what was disproved stay readable. **The event:** `{eventId, kind: assert|correct|retract, subject, supersedes: {previousEventId, previousDigest}, reason, oldValue, newValue, recordedAt, recordedBy: {role, identity}, evidence, authentication, signature}`. A value carries its own SHA-256 and the digest of the record that carried it; a correction binds both the predecessor event digest and the exact original value digest. **Append-only:** the log carries a `history` digest witness over every event and a `logDigest` over the history; an edited, deleted or reordered event is `CR_REFUSED_HISTORY_ERASED`. **Binding:** a correction must name both the predecessor id and its canonical digest (`CR_REFUSED_UNBOUND_CORRECTION`, `CR_REFUSED_PREDECESSOR_MISMATCH`), the exact original value (`CR_REFUSED_ORIGINAL_MISMATCH`) and the same subject (`CR_REFUSED_SUBJECT_CHANGED`); a different subject is a new assertion. **Chain:** one direct successor per predecessor (`CR_REFUSED_FORK`), no revisited event (`CR_REFUSED_CYCLE`, checked before fork because a cycle makes its predecessor look forked), one `assert` per subject (`CR_REFUSED_ASSERTION_EXISTS`), unique `eventId` (`CR_REFUSED_EVENT_ID_REUSED`), and a retraction only of an asserted subject (`CR_REFUSED_RETRACTION_UNBOUND`). **Propagation:** `derive_dependents` walks `evidence/`, `docs/`, `forensics/tools/` and `courts/` for the superseded value digest, record digest and `oldValue.citation`, records the citation line and the nearest supersession marker, and `propagate` refuses a dependent left `VALID` (`CR_REFUSED_INVALIDATION_NOT_PROPAGATED`) and a spurious invalidation (`CR_REFUSED_SPURIOUS_INVALIDATION`); the marker classification is reported, not proved. **Signatures:** every event and the log are signed with A10's test-only HMAC-SHA256 signer and labelled `SELF_ISSUED`; A14 records no declared identity, so **0 events are authenticated**. **16 stable codes**, each with a `control-only` fixture and a named test. **The real log:** 11 subjects / 22 events — 6 `CORRECTED`, 5 `RETRACTED`, 0 `DISPUTED`, 0 `UNKNOWN`; 7 `prose-only`, 4 `machine-readable-unsigned`, 0 signed. orchestrator commits). `docs/POLICY-ENGINE.md` is the normative document, `forensics/tools/policy_engine.py` the implementation, `forensics/tools/tests/test_policy_engine.py` 40 controls, and `evidence/a20/{POLICY,POLICY-PIN,DECISION,CONTROLS}.json` the records, produced deterministically by `evidence/a20/build_evidence.py`. **The rule:** the decision is a conjunction of obligations and never a score; the default is deny; an obligation whose record is absent, unreadable or unresolvable is `UNKNOWN` and the decision is `DENY` with `PE_REFUSED_OBLIGATION_UNEVALUATED`, never skipped; `UNKNOWN` is never `MET`; a required obligation recorded `NOT_APPLICABLE` is refused; two sources of one fact must agree; and the policy is pinned separately so that a candidate cannot choose the policy that judges it. **The policy record** is `{policyId, version, digest, default: "deny", scores: false, obligations, provenance}` with eight obligations, each naming `{tool, record, locator}`, a closed predicate language (`eq`, `none`, `count-at-least`, `all-in`, `all`) and optional corroboration; a score-shaped key or `scores` not false is `PE_REFUSED_SCORE`, an undeclared field `PE_REFUSED_POLICY_INVALID`, a non-deny default `PE_REFUSED_DEFAULT_ALLOW`. **The pin** records `{policyDigest, pinnedAt, pinnedBy, candidate}` separately: an unrecorded policy is `PE_REFUSED_POLICY_UNRECORDED`, an amended one `PE_REFUSED_POLICY_AMENDED`, one established or pinned by the candidate's own producer (read from A14's record, not asserted) `PE_REFUSED_POLICY_FROM_CANDIDATE`, an unknown candidate `PE_REFUSED_CANDIDATE_UNKNOWN`, and a waiver naming the candidate `PE_REFUSED_SELF_WAIVER`. **Determinism:** obligations are evaluated in ascending id order and the policy must record them in that order (`PE_REFUSED_ORDER`), a reordered decision is refused, output arrays and records are sorted, the decision carries no timestamp, two evaluations and two processes are byte-identical, and a differing pair is `PE_REFUSED_NONDETERMINISTIC`. **The real policy and decision:** eight obligations read A5, A6, A7, A12, A14, A15, A17 and A19; `A05.populations-admitted` (`MET`, 16 populations, 0 refused declarations keeping their declared state), `A06.suppression-member-identities` (**`UNMET`**, 8 of 11 records declare an `ACTIVE` suppression with no member identity), `A07.delta-resolution-conditions` (`MET`, 0 `RESOLVED` dispositions), `A12.independent-attestation` (**`UNMET`**, 0 of 4 independently verified), `A14.release-authorised` (**`UNMET`**, recorded outcome `REFUSED`), `A15.release-threshold-met` (**`UNMET`**, `UNREACHABLE`), `A17.forward-freeze-closed` (**`UNMET`**, state `OPEN` and `closed` false, the freeze and status records agreeing), `A19.retraction-propagated` (`MET`, 5 retractions, 0 valid dependents). The decision is **`DENY` with five unmet obligations and no refusal code** — every source was present and evaluable, so the denial is carried by the unmet obligations and nothing else, and the honest expected result is recorded rather than tuned away. **Controls:** 19 `control-only` fixtures, one per refusal code (16), a second order control and a positive; the required wrong conclusions are a score policy, a default-allow policy, an unevaluated obligation treated as a pass, an amended policy against its pin, a candidate-supplied policy, a self-waiver, a duplicate obligation, a reordered decision and a contradiction between two sources; the positive control's synthetic obligations all reach `MET` and reach `ADMIT`, which establishes that the procedure can admit, not that any real obligation is met. **Defects:** no defect was found in an existing record; the five unmet obligations are the records' own state, not a check to repair. **Release gate integrated:** item 9 runs the policy live and adds one row `RG_POLICY_DENIED`; the gate's real run in the court goes from **33 refusals to 34** with items 1–8 unchanged (19 `RG_CLAIM_UNCERTIFIABLE_EVIDENCE`, 11 `RG_CLAIM_HISTORICAL_EVIDENCE`, 1 `RG_PACKAGE_MISSING`, 1 `RG_AUTHORISATION_REFUSED`, 1 `RG_THRESHOLD_UNMET`), and the gate self-test from 25 to 29 cases. **Gates:** the whole tools discovery is **1,564 tests, 0 failures** (1,520 before this item; 40 policy controls plus 4 gate controls, none removed, none ignored); `docs_consistency.py` and `spec_guard.py check` are clean; every created JSON passes `python3 -m json.tool`; the producer is byte-identical across two runs and against the committed bytes; `policy_engine.py evaluate` exits 1 printing `decision: DENY codes=NONE` and the five unmet obligations, `verify-pin` exits 0 with `code=PE_OK`, `verify-decision` exits 0 with `code=PE_OK`. `free -g` reported 17 GiB available, below the 24 GiB build floor, so no build ran; orchestrator commits). `docs/VERIFICATION-SUMMARIES.md` is the normative document, `forensics/tools/verification_summary.py` the implementation, `forensics/tools/tests/test_verification_summary.py` 42 controls, and `evidence/a21/{SUMMARY,SUMMARY.envelope,RECONSTRUCTION,CONTROLS}.json` the records, produced deterministically by `evidence/a21/build_evidence.py`. **The rule:** a summary is produced after policy evaluation, every count in it is recounted from its obligation list and derivable from the cited sources, every source is cited by digest, and a consumer that cites the summary in place of the sources is refused. **The summary:** `summarise(decision, sources)` writes `{topLevel: "summary", replacesEvidence: false, decision, decisionDigest, obligations: [{id,state,sourcePath,sourceDigest}], counts, unmet, reconstructedFrom: [{path,sha256}]}`; the field set is closed, the decision digest is the SHA-256 of the canonical decision bytes (the in-toto subject), and the eight primary sources plus the A17 corroborating record make nine cited records. **The recount:** counts are incremented from the obligation states and the decision's own `met`/`unmet`/`unknown`/`notApplicable` arrays are never read; | A23 | Formal verification of the verifier: the smallest evidence-processing core; verify that required evidence cannot be omitted, signatures cannot imply semantic correctness, substitution cannot preserve acceptance | DONE (2026-11-24, base `7c2b5f25f`, clean `staging` at the start; orchestrator commits). `docs/VERIFIER-FORMALISATION.md` is the normative document, `forensics/tools/verifier_formalisation.py` the implementation, `forensics/tools/tests/test_verifier_formalisation.py` 51 controls, and `evidence/a23/{SPEC,DIFFERENTIAL,PROPERTIES,CONTROLS}.json` the records, produced deterministically by `evidence/a23/build_evidence.py`. **The rule:** a specification written by reading the implementation proves nothing, and a finite enumeration is not a theorem; so the rules are written first as a machine-readable object, an independent reference evaluator is implemented from that object and does not import the implementation, and the relation between the two is established by exhaustive enumeration over stated finite domains plus invariants. **Nine decision rules (VF-R1..VF-R9), each named and declared in `SPEC.json`:** the state vocabulary is closed (MET, UNMET, UNKNOWN, NOT_APPLICABLE); ADMIT iff the required set is non-empty and every required obligation is MET, with no score, weight, count, threshold or rank contributing; an UNKNOWN is a refusal whether or not the obligation is required (an unevaluated obligation is a machinery defect, never MET and never NOT_APPLICABLE); NOT_APPLICABLE on a required obligation denies; an empty required set denies; the decision is independent of canonical order; it is deterministic; a k-of-n threshold is MET iff the distinct established domains among verified, role-valid, fresh, non-candidate approvals reach k, with aliases collapsed by establishment record and one identity contributing one domain; a correction chain is admissible iff every binding rule holds (predecessor id exists, predecessor digest matches, one successor per event, no cycle, one root per subject, same subject, original value preserved). Eight correction sub-rules VF-C1..VF-C8 name the authority refusal each prevents. **The reference:** `reference_decide`, `reference_threshold_count` and `reference_chain_admissible` read `SPEC.json` and never import `policy_engine`, `thresholds` or `corrections`; the adapters import inside the adapter, `assert_reference_independent` checks globals and source text, and the control `reference-imports-implementation` proves the check fires. **Domains and results:** `decision-small` 585 (= 1 + 8 + 64 + 512) over 0..3 obligations and every required mask; `decision-policy` **65,536 = 4**8**, the whole input space of an eight-obligation decision function; `threshold` 9,100 (1,820 multisets of 0..4 approvals over 12 archetypes times (3 + 2) k values over two inventories); `correction` 386 (383 chains of length 0..3 over two subjects plus 3 cycle witnesses). **75,607 comparisons, 0 disagreements.** **Seven invariants on both evaluators over 512 cases:** determinism, MET-monotonicity, required-UNMET monotonicity, UNKNOWN never admits, required-inapplicable denies, order independence, and no-score (a non-required obligation in MET, UNMET or NOT_APPLICABLE never changes the decision given no UNKNOWN). **Controls:** 10, one per refusal code and four deliberately wrong references (UNKNOWN as MET, NOT_APPLICABLE as MET, an alias counted twice, a digest ignored); every wrong reference is detected with the exact input, both outputs and the violated rule. **Defect found in this item's own statement:** the first VF-P7 omitted its no-UNKNOWN condition and the enumeration produced `states = [MET, MET, UNKNOWN], required = [true, false, false]`; the property was corrected, the rule (VF-R3) was not changed. **Observation, not a claim:** under the digest rule a cycle is unsatisfiable, so the reference reports VF-C4 and the implementation refuses `CR_REFUSED_PREDECESSOR_MISMATCH`; both refuse, and the differential cannot exhibit a cycle that fails only VF-C4. **Gates:** `free -g` reported 14 GiB available, below the 24 GiB build floor, so no build ran and the running 6 GiB `openssl-rs-court` was reused; the whole tools discovery is **1,731 tests, 0 failures** (1,680 before this item, 51 new controls, none removed, none ignored); `docs_consistency.py` exits 0; `spec_guard.py check` is clean; the four JSON records pass `python3 -m json.tool`; the producer is byte-identical twice and against the committed bytes; `verifier_formalisation.py differential` exits 0 with four domains, 75,607 compared and 0 disagreements, and `properties` exits 0 with all seven holding. **NOT_RUN:** an independent reviewer or distinct trust domain (Article 5); any machine-checked theorem (TLA+, Coq, Lean, Kani) or source-to-binary correspondence (Article 7); re-running the A15, A19 and A20 suites; obligation states derived from real records rather than the abstract vector; k above the available domains, more than 12 archetypes or 4 approvals, chains longer than 3 with retract, signatures or roles, and the history witness; BOUNDARY, SEAL and the Rust suite. ## F. ETP Addendum B — Rekor v2 public transparency

Normative text: `docs/ETP-ADDENDUM-B.md` (clauses B.1–B.33). The queue below is
§B.30 of that addendum. Identifiers `B0`–`B8` are the addendum's own and are
distinct from the `Tn` timing items in section B.

Pinned baseline, read from upstream on 2026-11-22 and recorded in the addendum's
§B.3.2: `rekor-tiles` v2.4.2 (2026-11-10, `a30bd31`), v2.3.0 (DSSE entry type
dropped, DSSE published as `hashedrekord`), v2.1.0 (POSIX backend), v2.0.1 (log
witnessing); C2SP `tlog-tiles` v1.0.0, `tlog-checkpoint` v1.1.0 and
`tlog-cosignature` v1.1.0, all dated 2026-10-07. Three tensions are recorded in
§B.3.3 and are not resolved by preference: C2SP recommends ML-DSA-44 while the
implementation labels it experimental; a client version does not imply a v2
service (the inspected `signing_config` still advertised a v1 endpoint); and the
DSSE-to-`hashedrekord` digest must be the admitted client's, not a substitute
preimage.

| id | item | status |
|---|---|---|

A missing member is `RV_REFUSED_ARCHIVE_MISSING`; a flipped byte, an unbound manifest or any failed verification is `RV_REFUSED_RECONSTRUCTION_MISMATCH` / `RV_REFUSED_ARCHIVE_INVALID`. Reconstruction is proved not to read the working tree by copying the archive outside it and pointing the tool's repository root at a path that does not exist. **Recorded design change:** the first archive copied the two A19 events as members, which added their withdrawn *value* digest to a scanned file and made A19's live `PROPAGATION.json` stop reproducing (the scan classifies any file quoting a superseded digest as a dependent, including a faithful copy of the original record). The real index covers **11 subjects: 0 ACTIVE, 6 CORRECTED, 5 WITHDRAWN**. A non-active entry is bound to an event present in the log with the matching subject and kind at that digest (`RV_REFUSED_REVOCATION_UNBOUND`); a correction the state does not reflect is `RV_REFUSED_CORRECTION_UNPROPAGATED` (the version-21 state against the full log). **Anti-rollback** has three distinct rules: a decreasing version is `RV_REFUSED_ROLLBACK_OF_STATE`, an older state offered as current is `RV_REFUSED_REPLAY_AS_CURRENT`, two states at one version are `RV_REFUSED_STATE_CONFLICT`; a newer state is admitted only when it names the accepted state's digest as its `previous`. **The demonstration** uses `FIXTURE-2` (a five-leaf RFC 6962 fixture log: leaf 0 is the real frozen A9 `a8-shard-000` statement, leaf 1 is a **labelled fixture statement** built over the withdrawn record's bytes because no A9 statement covers an A19 subject, leaves 2–4 are synthetic test-only), published at leaf index 1 with a 3-element inclusion proof and a size-5 checkpoint (root `4b9b97a4…4946d6`) signed by A10's test-only HMAC key. `DEMONSTRATION.json` records the three facts at one version: the inclusion proof still verifies (`RV_OK`, leaf 1, size 5), `current(ETP0-CLM-032/verdict)` is `WITHDRAWN` at version 22 superseded by `A19-E22`, and a present-tense use is refused (`RV_REFUSED_WITHDRAWN_ACCEPTED`). A control shows that deleting the non-claims makes item 10 the 36th. **The schema carries a closed field.** `evidence/etp/claim.schema.json` adds optional `$defs/transparency` `{state: NOT_ADMITTED|ADMITTED|PUBLISHED, profile: public-good-rekor-v2, statementDigest, bundleDigest, publishedAt}`, `additionalProperties: false`, no free text; `ADMITTED` requires profile and both digests, `PUBLISHED` also requires `publishedAt`; `authority.kind: public-transparency-log` requires the field. **Population is 0 of 35 claims and no claim was rewritten**, so the absence check is **VACUOUS** today — recorded, not reported as coverage (`evidence/b8/SCHEMA.json`). **The public non-claims are written and checked.** `docs/TRANSPARENCY-NON-CLAIMS.md` states no admitted Rekor service, no witness, no trusted timestamp, no publication and no independent reproduction, each with the B0/B2/B4/B5/B7 record that shows it and the §B.29 SEAL/EXTERNAL clause that requires the list; a missing row is `CE_REFUSED_NONCLAIMS_ABSENT`. **Two applications are pending, not done.** `forensics/etp8/agents-patch.diff` (additive §24) is `PENDING_MAINTAINER_APPLICATION`; `forensics/etp8/ci-transparency-job.patch` is `PENDING_CREDENTIAL` (the pushing OAuth app lacks the `workflow` scope, as for `forensics/etp7/ci-assurance-job.patch`). Both `git apply --check` clean and are recorded with digests in `evidence/b8/PENDING.json`. **The §10 mapping was attempted and refused.** Four rows for gate items 7–10 were added and `docs_consistency.py` refused `ET_GATE_ITEM_DRIFT` because `evidence/etp-t14/ARTICLES.json` registers gate-1..gate-6 only; editing that record is another item's evidence, so the edit was reverted (`forensics/etp8/release-gates-section10-attempt.diff`) and the integration is a documented pending obligation. The checker was not weakened. `evidence/b8/{ENFORCEMENT,SCHEMA,PENDING,CONTROLS}.json` and `build_evidence.py`; `forensics/tools/tests/test_continuous_enforcement.py` (43 tests) and five `TransparencyRow` tests in `test_release_gate.py`; `docs/CONTINUOUS-ENFORCEMENT.md`. Controls: 10 control-only rows, one per code plus an honest `NOT_ADMITTED` positive and `CE_REFUSED_USAGE`, 10/10 fired. Gates: whole tools discovery 2,482 tests, 9 failures, all the same cause: the required schema edit changes a member of the A11 and A16 frozen populations, so those tests refuse `PH_REFUSED_POPULATION_DRIFT` (the 2,434 pre-existing tests are present, none removed, none ignored; the 48 B8 tests pass); `docs_consistency.py` OK after the revert; `spec_guard.py check` 0 unclassified; `json.tool` on every record; producer twice byte-identical and `--check` clean; `release_gate.py --self-test` 34 cases OK; `git status` only this item. **Retained:** the A11/A16 frozen populations must be re-frozen at the commit that carries the schema extension; this non-committing batch may not write another item's evidence and cannot bind a record to an uncommitted candidate, so the drift is recorded rather than repaired; a passing item 10 is not a transparency result; the enforcement-regression rule is a single-edit tripwire, not tamper-proofing; the schema absence check is vacuous. **`NOT_RUN`:** any admitted log, witness, TSA, publication, archival, independent reproduction, applied `AGENTS.md`/CI integration, `BOUNDARY`, `SEAL`, Rust suite. Forward-freeze member `b8-receipt` (`a receipt records the enforced integration and its non-claims`) is satisfied; the freeze stays `OPEN`. ## H. Execution sequence (fixed)

Work proceeds strictly in item order. No choosing, no reordering, no skipping an
item to take a convenient one. Each item is finished (proof, controls, gates,
receipt) before the next begins, and each is pushed before the next starts.

| order | section | items |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | B — timing safety | `T1`, `T2`, `T3`, `T4`, `T5`, … `T22` |
| 2 | C — OpenSSH interoperability | `O1`, `O2`, … `O12` |
| 3 | D — ETP constitution and claim integrity | `ETP-0`, `ETP-1`, … `ETP-T15` |
| 4 | E — SARIF / DSSE addendum | `A0`, `A1`, … `A27` |
| 5 | F — ETP Addendum B (Rekor v2 transparency) | `B0`, `B1`, … `B8` |
| 6 | G — curl consumer | `C1` … `C5`, **last** |

The position of section F is an explicit override, recorded rather than implied:
Addendum B was admitted ahead of the remaining B/E items on instruction, and its
`B0`–`B8` run to completion in that order before the queue resumes at the item it
was interrupted on. The override does not move the curl consumer, which stays
last, and it does not discharge any open item in sections B–E.

The Addendum B position was then superseded again on instruction. The ordering
that now binds, and that is the authoritative one in this file, is:

1. sections B and C — timing safety and OpenSSH interoperability;
2. section D — ETP constitution and claim integrity;
3. section E — Addendum A (SARIF / DSSE);
4. section F — Addendum B (Rekor v2) and its `B0`–`B8`;
5. section J — the Performance Frontier Programme;
6. section K — Formal Refinement and Certification, **last**;
7. section G — the curl consumer, which remains the final consumer item.

The queue resumes at the item it was interrupted on (`ETP-3`). Each ordering
change is recorded here rather than implied, because a normative execution
policy needs an authoritative inventory and a traceable history of its own
changes; the enumerations in this file are reconciled against the affected rows
at every checkpoint.

An item that is `BLOCKED` is recorded with its blocker and the next item in the
same section begins; it is revisited when the blocker changes. Nothing is
declared complete without the evidence named in its row.

## I. Non-claims that bind this queue

SARIF compliance does not prove absence of vulnerabilities. A clean scanner
output is not memory safety. A signed report is not necessarily true. DSSE does
not establish independence. A transparency entry does not establish
completeness. SLSA provenance alone does not establish a reproducible build. A
reproducible build is not a correct build. Two signatures are not necessarily
two independent verifications. A cryptographically valid historical signature
does not establish present authorisation. A signed summary does not replace the
underlying proof. A successful CI workflow is not regulatory certification.

Addendum B (§B.32) adds: transparency inclusion does not prove software
correctness; one signed checkpoint is not a globally consistent history; two log
instances are not two independent operators; a witness has not reviewed the
implementation; witnessing does not prove all evidence was disclosed; a
timestamp does not prove an experiment occurred; a public commitment does not
establish availability of its payload; a transparency log is not a revocation
system; an authentic historical assertion may be currently invalid; experimental
post-quantum features are not production post-quantum security; registration in a
public-good log is not endorsement by its operator.

---

## J. Performance Frontier Programme (separate queue)

Normative programme: `docs/PERFORMANCE-FRONTIER.md`. Work queue:
`docs/PERFORMANCE-FRONTIER-TODO.md`, items `PFP-0` … `PFP-17`.

This is a separate queue because it is a distinct concern with its own governing
standard, not a re-lettering of sections B–G. Its bindings on this queue are:

- every performance result is an ETP claim under `docs/ETP-CLAIM-ALGEBRA.md` and
  carries a candidate identity, instrument identity, population, scope, threat
  model and obligations;
- performance classifications (`MEASURED`, `COMPETITIVE`, `PARITY_ESTABLISHED`,
  `AHEAD`, `FRONTIER_LEADING`, `INDEPENDENTLY_REPRODUCED_FRONTIER`) are disjoint
  from `SAFE_COMPATIBLE` and from semantic assurance;
- a faster arm without the differential courts and the binary constant-time
  audit at the same digest is an experiment, not a candidate;
- `PFP-1` and `PFP-2` overlap the open timing obligations T23, T24 and T29's
  residuals; a performance result never discharges a timing obligation, and a
  timing obligation is never traded for speed;
- a result measured on the virtualised court is `SCOPED_OBSERVATION` and is not
  a frontier result; `FRONTIER_LEADING` requires bare metal with a PMU on every
  platform the claim covers.

Nothing in section J alters the curl consumer's position as last.

---

## K. Formal Refinement and Certification (separate queue)

Normative programme: `docs/FORMAL-REFINEMENT-CERTIFICATION.md`. Work queue:
`docs/FRC-TODO.md`, items `FRC-0` … `FRC-10`.

A third separate queue, for the same reason as section J: a distinct concern
with its own standard. Its bindings on this queue:

- every FRC result is an ETP claim and inherits the claim algebra's refusal
  rules; an FRC item never upgrades an ETP verdict by itself, it supplies
  evidence the verdict may cite;
- matching the frontier is a precondition for claiming to exceed it, and the
  property matched and the property added must both be named;
- a proof in a model is not a proof about the deployed binary, and a binary
  timing result is not a physical side-channel guarantee;
- a specification is never generated by restating the implementation, and no
  obligation is closed by weakening a requirement;
- DO-178C objective satisfaction is recorded as such and never as certification;
- `PFP` optimisation and `FRC` evidence interact in one direction: an
  optimisation may proceed only while the FRC evidence for the property it could
  affect remains valid at the new digest, and any change to a named kernel,
  specification or toolchain invalidates the claim.

Section K runs after every item in sections D, E, F and J. It is the final
assurance track, not an interleaved one, because its frontier-gap register and
specification hierarchy consume their outputs. The FRC queue therefore starts
empty of execution and is picked up when sections D, E, F and J are complete.
The curl consumer remains the final consumer item.
